# Deep review — recurrent multi-agent audit (2026-09-25)

A three-round recurrent review of every module in `src/`, run with 151 agents (10 module-group
reviewers per round, an adversarial verifier per bug claim, a pragmatic-maintainer judge per
suggestion batch). Every module was compiled with the new CHICKEN 6.0.1pre1 `csc` under
`~/.local/bin` into a scratch repository, so all bug claims below were **verified by executing
repros against the compiled modules** (except `aux.judy`/`aux.lua`/`aux.hwy`, reviewed statically —
their external headers are absent on this machine).

**Confirmed bugs: 92** (7 critical, 31 major, 54 minor) · 
**Kept suggestions: 37** · 1 claim refuted in verification, 25 suggestions rejected by the judge.

## Confirmed bugs by module

### aux.anthropic.scm

#### [CRITICAL] `aux.anthropic.scm:300` — Validator passes double-overflow numbers (1e309); simdjson SIGABRTs

Same gate, second hole: the `number` production (lines 300-313) validates only the JSON number grammar. simdjson throws NUMBER_ERROR for any number whose value rounds to +/-infinity in double (e.g. 1e309, -1e999, 1e999), so {"a":1e309} passes anthropic-json/well-formed? and anthropic-json/parse aborts the process with an uncatchable SIGABRT, from any response body, error body or SSE frame. Fix: after matching a number, bound its magnitude -- e.g. compute the effective decimal exponent (integer-digit count + explicit exponent, adjusted for the fraction) and return #f when it exceeds 308 (mantissa non-zero); or, cheaper and safe, reject any number literal whose explicit exponent field parses to > 308 or < -400 and whose integer part is longer than 309 digits.

**Evidence.** Ran: bodies {"a":1e999}, {"a":1e309} and {"a":-1e999} each print well-formed? #t and then 'terminate called ... NUMBER_ERROR: Problem while parsing a number', Aborted (core dumped), exit 134.

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.anthropic.scm: the `number` production inside anthropic-json/well-formed? (lines ~300-313) checks only the JSON number grammar (sign, digits, optional fraction, optional exponent digits) and never bounds magnitude. Reproduced with the compiled module: csi -s repro-1e309.scm on body {"a":1e309} printed `well-formed? #t`, then `terminate called after throwing an instance of 'simdjson::simdjson_error' / what(): NUMBER_ERROR: Problem while parsing a number / Aborted (core dumped)`, EXIT=134. Same abort reproduced for {"a":-1e999}. Callers confirm remote reachability: anthropic-response/decode (line 1122), anthropic-raise/api (line 191) and the SSE frame paths (lines 1416, 1503) pass network response bodies through this gate straight into anthropic-json/parse, and src/chicken-simdjson.cpp has no try/catch, so the throw reaches std::terminate.

#### [CRITICAL] `aux.anthropic.scm:328` — Validator passes unpaired surrogate escapes; simdjson SIGABRTs

anthropic-json/well-formed? exists solely to guarantee that no body ever reaches simdjson-parse-ondemand-callback with content it would throw on (chicken-simdjson.cpp has no try/catch, so a simdjson_error reaches std::terminate and kills the process with an uncatchable SIGABRT). The \u escape branch (lines 327-333) only checks that the four following characters are hex digits; it never checks surrogate pairing. simdjson requires \uD800-\uDBFF to be followed by a \uDC00-\uDFFF low escape (and rejects a lone low surrogate), throwing STRING_ERROR otherwise. So the body {"a":"\ud800"} (and {"a":"\udc00"}) passes the validator and anthropic-json/parse aborts the whole process. Every parse in the module is downstream of this gate -- response bodies (anthropic-response/decode), error bodies (anthropic-raise/api), and every SSE data frame (anthropic-sse/frame) -- so one broken or hostile proxy page or SSE event kills the client uncatchably, which is exactly what the header comment says the gate prevents ('That gate is load bearing'). Fix: in the \u branch, decode the 4 hex digits to an integer u; if u is in #xD800-#xDBFF require the next 6 chars to be a \uDC00-\uDFFF escape (and consume both); if u is in #xDC00-#xDFFF alone, return #f.

**Evidence.** Ran: (anthropic-json/parse "{\"a\":\"\\ud800\"}") after (anthropic-json/well-formed? ...) printed #t -> 'terminate called after throwing an instance of simdjson::simdjson_error, what(): STRING_ERROR', process Aborted (core dumped), exit 134. Control: the valid pair \ud83d\ude00 parses fine to the emoji, and a lone \udc00 also aborts. csi 6.0.1pre1, module compiled -O2.

**Independent verification.** Code inspection of /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.anthropic.scm: the \u branch in `text` inside `anthropic-json/well-formed?` (the `((char=? e #\u) ...)` clause, ~line 328) only verifies bounds and four hex chars via `anthropic-char/hex?`; there is no surrogate-pairing logic anywhere (grep for surrogate/ud800/udc00 across src and src/test finds nothing). chicken-simdjson.cpp has no try/catch (grep confirms), and the module exports `*`, so both procedures are directly testable. Repro with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi against the pre-compiled scratch repo: (anthropic-json/well-formed? "{\"a\":\"\\ud800\"}") => #t, same for \udc00 => #t; control (anthropic-json/parse "{\"a\":\"\\ud83d\\ude00\"}") => ((a 😀)), exit 0. Then (anthropic-json/parse "{\"a\":\"\\ud800\"}") and separately "{\"a\":\"\\udc00\"}" both print 'terminate called after throwing an instance of simdjson::simdjson_error, what(): STRING_ERROR: Problem while parsing a string', Aborted (core dumped), exit 134 — an uncatchable SIGABRT, exactly as claimed.

#### [CRITICAL] `aux.anthropic.scm:335` — Invalid raw UTF-8 in body passes validator; simdjson SIGABRTs

Same gate, third hole: when a response body contains bytes that are not valid UTF-8 (a proxy error page in Latin-1 is the realistic case), CHICKEN 6 decodes each invalid byte B into the surrogate-escape codepoint #xDC00+B (verified: byte 0xC3 in {"a":"\xc3("} reads back as char #xDCC3). Those chars are >= #\space, so the string production's only content check at line 335 ((char<? c #\space)) accepts them and well-formed? answers #t. The FFI then hands simdjson the original invalid bytes and it throws UTF8_ERROR -> std::terminate -> SIGABRT, uncatchable. Fix is cheap because of the surrogate-escape representation: in the string scanner's else branch (line 336), also reject any char whose codepoint lies in #xD800-#xDFFF -- that single check closes this hole entirely (raw lone surrogates cannot otherwise occur in a decoded CHICKEN string), and it complements the \u pairing check of the other finding.

**Evidence.** Ran: wrote the 8 bytes 7b 22 61 22 3a 22 c3 28 22 7d ({"a":"<0xC3>("}) to a file, read it with read-string, well-formed? answered #t, anthropic-json/parse -> 'terminate called ... UTF8_ERROR: The input is not valid UTF-8', Aborted, exit 134. Codepoint dump of the read string: 7b 22 61 22 3a 22 dcc3 28 22 7d, confirming the #xDCxx representation the fix keys on.

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.anthropic.scm lines 287-379: the string production's only content check is line 335 ((char<? c #\space) #f); everything >= #\space is accepted at line 336. Reproduced exactly as claimed: wrote the 10 bytes 7b 22 61 22 3a 22 c3 28 22 7d to a scratchpad file, read it with read-string under the custom csi (/home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi, CHICKEN_REPOSITORY_PATH pointing at the pre-built auxrepo). Output: `codepoints: 7b 22 61 22 3a 22 dcc3 28 22 7d` (the invalid byte 0xC3 decodes to the surrogate-escape #xDCC3, which is >= #\space), then `well-formed? => #t`, then `(anthropic-json/parse s)` -> `terminate called after throwing an instance of 'simdjson::simdjson_error' / what(): UTF8_ERROR: The input is not valid UTF-8 / Aborted (core dumped)`, exit 134. So the validator passes a body that then makes the process die on an uncatchable C++ exception.

#### [CRITICAL] `aux.anthropic.scm:857` — Body write to a dead transport child dies of SIGPIPE, leaking the API-key file

anthropic-transport/curl writes the request body with (display body stdin) after spawning the child. If the child exits before reading stdin and the body exceeds the 64K pipe buffer, the write gets SIGPIPE, whose default disposition kills the entire csi/compiled process (exit 141). No handle-exceptions runs, and the module's own on-exit sweep is documented (line ~711) as NOT running on signal death, so the 0600 header file containing the raw x-api-key is left on disk permanently. This is precisely the failure the module already diagnoses as transport exit 126/127 ('the transport program could not be executed') at lines 641-642 -- a mistyped anthropic/curl path -- but that diagnosis is unreachable whenever the body is large (a long transcript or big tool schemas easily exceeds 64K). Fix: ignore SIGPIPE (or install a no-op handler via (chicken process signal), which is already imported) around the stdin write, and wrap the (display body stdin)/(close-output-port stdin) pair in handle-exceptions that falls through to the existing status-#f path, which already closes the wire, deletes the key file and raises the properly diagnosed anthropic-transport-error. Note that merely ignoring SIGPIPE is not enough for a clean report: the EPIPE then surfaces as a raw i/o error (kind #f), not an anthropic-transport-error, so the write error must be swallowed and the no-status branch left to do the raise.

**Evidence.** Repro (scratchpad/probe6-sigpipe.scm): parameterize anthropic/curl to "/bin/false" (or "/no/such/curl-binary", the realistic exec-failure case), body = 200KB user message, call anthropic/messages inside handle-exceptions. Observed: process killed, shell reports exit 141 (128+SIGPIPE), nothing printed, and $TMPDIR/anthropic-<96bit>.headers survives containing 'x-api-key: sk-ant-test-0123456789'. Same run with a SIGPIPE handler installed: survives, temp file deleted, but condition is a raw EPIPE error (anthropic-error kind #f), confirming the fall-through-to-close fix is also needed for correct diagnosis.

**Independent verification.** Reproduced end-to-end with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi against the pre-compiled aux repo. (1) Primitive probe (scratchpad/probe-pipe-raw.scm): process* "/bin/false", display of a 200000-char string to its stdin -> shell EXIT=141 (128+SIGPIPE), nothing printed. (2) Full API repro (scratchpad/probe6-sigpipe.scm): (anthropic/curl "/bin/false") and also "/no/such/curl-binary", 200KB user message, anthropic/messages wrapped in caller handle-exceptions -> EXIT=141, handler never ran, and $TMPDIR/anthropic-1af9acada14765f9c8e8cddc.headers survived containing "x-api-key: sk-ant-test-0123456789" (0600). (3) Control: same missing binary with a small body -> proper anthropic transport failure, exit-status 126, diagnosis "the transport program could not be executed", temp file deleted, EXIT=0 — so the documented 126/127 diagnosis is unreachable precisely when body > pipe buffer. (4) With set-signal-handler! on signal/pipe: process survives, temp file deleted by the existing line-852 handler, but the condition is raw (exn errno 32 file-error "cannot write - Broken pipe"), anthropic-transport-error? => #f, with the call chain naming aux.anthropic.scm:857 scheme#display — confirming both the fault location and that ignoring SIGPIPE alone is insufficient for correct diagnosis. Root cause corroborated in chicken-core: only tcp.scm:234 installs signal(SIGPIPE, SIG_IGN) (at tcp-unit load), and aux.anthropic.scm never loads tcp nor mentions SIGPIPE (grep empty).

#### [MAJOR] `aux.anthropic.scm:287` — Validator has no nesting-depth limit; deep arrays segfault simdjson

anthropic-json/well-formed? recurses through value/array/object with no depth bound, and CHICKEN's stack handling lets it validate arbitrarily deep documents (40,000-deep nested arrays validate fine). But the C++ side it is gating does not survive what it approves: simdjson-parse-ondemand-callback segfaults (SIGSEGV) on the same input the validator just passed. Measured threshold on this build: 20,000-deep '[[[...0...]]]' parses, 40,000-deep segfaults the process (exit 139). So a hostile or broken server body of ~80 KB of '[' kills the client process outright -- the validator's whole purpose (per its own comment block) is to make that impossible. Fix: thread a depth counter through value/array/object in the validator and reject documents nested deeper than a safe constant well under simdjson's limits (e.g. 512); alternatively (or additionally) configure the simdjson parser's max_depth and catch the error in chicken-simdjson.cpp -- but the pure-Scheme gate fix requires no C++ rebuild.

**Evidence.** csi with the compiled module: (anthropic-json/well-formed? (deep 40000)) => #t, exit 0, where (deep n) is n '['s + "0" + n ']'s. Isolated the crashing stage: (simdjson-parse-ondemand-callback s (anthropic-json/utf8-length s)) on (deep 40000) => 'Segmentation fault (core dumped)', exit=139; (deep 20000) parses fine; full (anthropic-json/parse (deep 100000)) also segfaults, exit=139.

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.anthropic.scm:287-383: anthropic-json/well-formed? recurses through value/array/object with no depth counter, exactly as claimed. chicken-simdjson.cpp's chicken_simdjson_visit_ondemand recurses in C++ per nesting level with no try/catch. Reproduced with the compiled module (scratch repo, /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi): script builds (deep n) = n '['s + "0" + n ']'s, checks well-formed?, then calls anthropic-json/parse. n=500: well-formed? => #t, parsed ok, exit=0. n=20000: #t, parsed ok, exit=0. n=40000: well-formed? => #t printed, then 'Segmentation fault (core dumped)', exit=139 — matching the reviewer's thresholds exactly. The module's own comment block (lines 275-278) states the validator exists precisely so that no input that kills the process ever reaches simdjson ('this is a complete validator, and nothing is parsed without it'), so this is a contract violation of the module's stated design, not intended behavior. All parse call sites (lines 191, 1124, 1416, 1503-1504) gate on well-formed? and would pass a deep body straight into the segfaulting parse.

#### [MAJOR] `aux.anthropic.scm:293` — Validator treats Unicode whitespace as JSON whitespace; form feed SIGABRTs

The `skip` helper of anthropic-json/well-formed? (line 293) uses char-whitespace?, which in CHICKEN 6 is Unicode-aware and accepts U+000B (VT), U+000C (FF), U+00A0 (NBSP), U+2028 (LS) etc. JSON whitespace is only space, tab, LF and CR. So a body with any of these characters between tokens passes the gate that exists solely to keep malformed input away from the try/catch-free simdjson binding. Consequences measured: a form feed between tokens ('[1,\f2]') passes the validator and then simdjson throws TAPE_ERROR -> std::terminate -> SIGABRT, killing the whole process -- exactly the failure mode the validator's header comment says it is load-bearing against. VT, NBSP and U+2028 pass the validator and then die inside simdjson->scheme with a generic '(exn) Unknown simdjson type' error that is not an anthropic-error condition (so the module's own condition discipline is broken) and leaks the malloc'd dom. Fix: replace char-whitespace? in `skip` with an explicit (memv c '(#\space #\tab #\newline #\return)) (or the equivalent code-point test). anthropic-string/trim's use of char-whitespace? is fine and should stay.

**Evidence.** csi with the compiled module: (anthropic-json/well-formed? "[1,\x0C;2]") => #t for chars 11, 12, 160, 8232 (all report char-whitespace? #t). Then in separate processes: (anthropic-json/parse "[1,<char>2]") -- char 12: 'terminate called after throwing an instance of simdjson::simdjson_error, what(): TAPE_ERROR', shell reports 'Aborted (core dumped)', exit=134. Chars 11/160/8232: exit=70 with 'Error: ... Unknown simdjson type' raised from aux.simdjson.scm:82, and condition-case shows anthropic-error? #f on the caught condition.

**Independent verification.** Read src/aux.anthropic.scm: line 293 `skip` uses char-whitespace? exactly as claimed; module header (line ~275) states the validator is complete and load-bearing because the simdjson binding has no try/catch. Ran compiled module via /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi with the scratch repo: (anthropic-json/well-formed? "[1,<c>2]") => #t for code points 11, 12, 160, 8232 (all char-whitespace? #t; none is RFC 8259 JSON whitespace). Then anthropic-json/parse in separate processes: cp=12 (form feed) => "terminate called after throwing an instance of 'simdjson::simdjson_error' what(): TAPE_ERROR", Aborted (core dumped), exit=134, not caught by condition-case; cp=11/160/8232 => caught plain (exn) with anthropic-error? #f, so the module's own condition discipline is broken. Tests (src/test/anthropic.scm:415-432) assert strict rejection of similar near-miss inputs, confirming this is contrary to intent, not documented behavior.

#### [MAJOR] `aux.anthropic.scm:1499` — Empty partial_json accumulation flags spurious INVALID_JSON

anthropic-sse-block->json treats a tool_use block as 'no JSON streamed' only when the fragment LIST is null (line 1499: (null? (anthropic-sse-block-json blk))). But the real API's documented streams send an initial input_json_delta with "partial_json":"" -- for a parameterless tool that empty fragment is the ONLY delta. The accumulation is then the list (""), raw is "", well-formed? is #f, and the block is recorded in state-errors as an input error with raw "". Downstream, anthropic-tool-use->result answers that tool_use with an is_error tool_result claiming INVALID_JSON instead of running the tool, so every streamed no-argument tool call fails. The test suite (test/anthropic.scm:250) covers only the no-delta-at-all shape, which is why this was missed. Fix: mirror the official SDKs' `if json_buf:` guard -- treat an accumulation whose concatenation is "" exactly like the null-fragment case (keep the template's input), e.g. test (string=? "" raw) instead of / in addition to the null? test, reusing the existing text-fallback sub-branch.

**Evidence.** Ran: fed anthropic-sse->message a stream with content_block_start {type tool_use, input {}}, one input_json_delta with partial_json "", content_block_stop, message_delta, message_stop. Result: input-errors ((tu_1 "")) and the block's input forced to '() -- i.e. the tool call is marked broken. With no delta at all (the suite's shape) input-errors is ().

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.anthropic.scm:1486-1509 — the claim matches the code exactly: the "no JSON streamed" branch is gated on (null? (anthropic-sse-block-json blk)), so a single input_json_delta with partial_json "" makes the list (""), raw "", and (anthropic-json/well-formed? "") is #f, pushing (id "") onto state-errors and forcing input to '(). Reproduced with the specified csi: wrote scratchpad/repro-empty-partial.scm feeding anthropic-sse->message a stream identical to the suite's parameterless-tool fixture plus one {"type":"input_json_delta","partial_json":""} delta. Output: `errors: ((toolu_e1 ))` (i.e. ((\"toolu_e1\" \"\"))), `content: #(((type tool_use) (id toolu_e1) (name now) (input ())))`, `well-formed? "" -> #f`. Downstream confirmed at lines 1690-1701: anthropic-tool-use->result does (assoc id input-errors) and, when found, returns an is_error tool_result with INVALID_JSON instead of applying the tool. External corroboration: (1) the official streaming docs (platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/streaming) show full tool-use streams whose FIRST input_json_delta is {"partial_json":""} — fetched page, lines 938 and 1432 of saved output; (2) anthropic-sdk-python's accumulator (_messages.py) guards with `if json_buf:` before parsing, so an empty-only accumulation keeps the template's input {} — exactly the guard aux.scm lacks. The suite's fixture/sse/no-input (test/anthropic.scm ~252) covers only the zero-delta shape, and its comment asserts parameterless tools send NO delta, which the docs' initial-empty-delta pattern contradicts.

#### [MINOR] `aux.anthropic.scm:401` — Huge integer literal escapes gate as unclassified error and leaks dom

A JSON body containing an integer literal too large for int64/double classification (e.g. a 30-digit or 400-digit integer) passes anthropic-json/well-formed? but simdjson->scheme hits the (else (error "Unknown simdjson type" ...)) branch at aux.simdjson.scm:82. Two consequences inside this module: (1) the raise is a bare (exn), not an anthropic-decode-error, so it escapes anthropic-send's (c (anthropic-error) ...) condition-case raw and callers who handle the module's documented condition taxonomy miss it; (2) in anthropic-json/parse (lines 404-407) the raise happens between simdjson-parse-ondemand-callback and simdjson-free, so the dom is leaked. Fix: wrap the simdjson->scheme call in anthropic-json/parse so the dom is freed on escape and the error is re-signalled via anthropic-raise/decode; optionally also bound integer-digit runs in the validator.

**Evidence.** Ran: {"a":1<400 zeros>} and {"a":123456789012345678901234567890} both answer well-formed? #t and then raise from aux.simdjson.scm:82 chicken.base#error ('Unknown simdjson type'), exit 70 -- a plain exn, not an anthropic-* condition; simdjson-free at aux.anthropic.scm:406 is skipped on that path.

**Independent verification.** Reproduced with the compiled modules. Script under the scratchpad importing (aux anthropic), run with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi -q -s, output:

well-formed? 30-digit: #t
well-formed? 401-digit: #t
30-digit -> BARE (exn), NOT anthropic-error: Unknown simdjson type
401-digit -> BARE (exn), NOT anthropic-error: Unknown simdjson type

Both {"a":123456789012345678901234567890} and {"a":1<400 zeros>} pass anthropic-json/well-formed? (its `number` production accepts unbounded digit runs, /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.anthropic.scm lines ~300-313) and then raise chicken.base#error "Unknown simdjson type" from the (else ...) branch of simdjson->scheme (aux.simdjson.scm line 82). A condition-case with a (c (anthropic-error) ...) clause does NOT catch it; only the bare (exn) clause does — so it escapes the module's documented taxonomy (the comment at aux.anthropic.scm:140-142 promises "Everything this module signals is a compound condition of kinds (exn anthropic-error KIND)", which this path violates).

Root cause verified in C sources: chicken-simdjson.cpp lines 72-93 map only ondemand::number_type::{signed_integer, unsigned_integer, floating_point_number}; simdjson.h (line 14736, 16298-16306) classifies any integer literal >19 digits (or 19 digits > 9223372036854775807) as number_type::big_integer, which the wrapper leaves as CHICKEN_SIMDJSON_TYPE_UNKNOWN — hitting simdjson->scheme's else branch.

Dom leak verified by reading anthropic-json/parse (aux.anthropic.scm lines 402-407): simdjson-free sits after the simdjson->scheme call in a plain let* with no dynamic-wind/handle-exceptions, so on this raise the dom is never freed. (Note simdjson-parse/ondemand and simdjson-load/ondemand in aux.simdjson.scm have the same leak shape.)

No test in src/test/anthropic.scm covers big integers, and no comment marks this as intended.

#### [MINOR] `aux.anthropic.scm:514` — anthropic-json/set matches keys with eq?: string keys in `extra` duplicate instead of merging

anthropic-json/set decides replace-vs-append with (eq? key (car (car o))). That is only correct for interned symbols. The encoder explicitly supports STRING object keys (line 482), and the request-building comment (lines 1066-1070) promises that `extra` 'is MERGED, not appended, so effort: "high" extra: '((output_config ...)) produces one output_config, not two (a duplicate key is legal JSON and the server keeps the last one, silently dropping the first)'. A caller who spells an extra key as a string -- natural, since the encoder accepts it everywhere else -- gets exactly the silently-duplicated key the comment warns about: two equal string keys never eq?-match, so anthropic-json/merge appends a second copy and the emitted body carries the key twice. The same applies to two successive sets of the same string key. Fix: compare with equal?, or normalize keys to symbols at the top of anthropic-json/set (mirroring how anthropic-json/ref documents that keys are interned symbols).

**Evidence.** (anthropic-json/write (anthropic-json/set (anthropic-json/set '() "output_config" 1) "output_config" 2)) => {"output_config":1,"output_config":2}; likewise (anthropic-json/merge '((model "m")) '(("model" "x"))) => {"model":"m","model":"x"} -- the server would keep "x" and silently drop the caller's intent to override, or vice versa depending on which side used strings. Run under the scratch repository csi.

**Independent verification.** Read src/aux.anthropic.scm: anthropic-json/set (lines 512-517) uses (eq? key (car (car o))); the encoder's object branch (lines 480-483) explicitly accepts string keys ("a JSON object key must be a symbol or a string"); the request-building comment (lines 1066-1070) promises `extra` "is MERGED, not appended" to avoid duplicate keys. Reproduced under the scratch-repo csi (CHICKEN_REPOSITORY_PATH set, /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi): (anthropic-json/write (anthropic-json/set (anthropic-json/set '() "output_config" 1) "output_config" 2)) => {"output_config":1,"output_config":2}; (anthropic-json/merge '((model "m")) '(("model" "x")))'s write => {"model":"m","model":"x"}; mixed case (anthropic-json/merge '((model "claude") (max_tokens 100)) '(("max_tokens" 5))) => {"model":"claude","max_tokens":100,"max_tokens":5} — exactly the silent duplicate the module's own comment warns the server resolves by dropping one side. Tests (src/test/anthropic.scm:521-525) cover only symbol keys.

#### [MINOR] `aux.anthropic.scm:933` — Blocking drain has no unwind: a raise from the body read leaks the wire

anthropic/stream carefully wraps its SSE fold in dynamic-wind ('the dynamic-wind is load bearing ... without it the curl child and the 0600 file holding the API key would leak', lines 1620-1622), but the BLOCKING path has no equivalent guard: anthropic-wire/drain! (lines 933-939) calls (anthropic-port->string (anthropic-wire-port w)) and only closes the wire afterwards. If read-string raises -- EIO on the pipe, a srfi-18 thread termination, or any custom transport port whose reader raises -- the close thunk never runs. anthropic-send's condition-case (line 972-979) catches only (anthropic-error), so the raw error escapes with the wire still open: for the curl backend that is a live/zombie child and three descriptors per failed request until process exit (the header file itself is already deleted at first response header, so only fds/child leak). Both the 2xx path (anthropic/messages -> anthropic-wire->body!) and the non-2xx path inside anthropic-send are affected. Fix: wrap the read in anthropic-wire/drain! with the same dynamic-wind/close discipline the streaming path uses (close in the after-thunk, then re-check exit status), which is safe because anthropic-wire/close! is already idempotent by memoisation.

**Evidence.** Repro (scratchpad/probe2-drain-leak.scm): a transport returning a 200 wire whose make-input-port reader raises 'simulated EIO on body read', close thunk increments a counter. anthropic/messages raises the EIO error and the counter stays 0 (expected 1). Same for a 500 wire drained inside anthropic-send: counter 0. Output: 'wire closes after the raise (should be 1, is): 0' on both paths.

**Independent verification.** Reproduced with a scratchpad script (scratchpad/verify-drain-leak.scm) run under /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi against the compiled (aux anthropic): a transport returning a wire whose make-input-port reader raises, with a close thunk that increments a counter. Output:
  200 path raised: simulated EIO on body read
  closes after 200-path raise (claim says 0): 0
  500 path raised: simulated EIO on body read
  closes after 500-path raise (claim says 0): 0
  control: closes after clean drain (should be 1): 1
Code reading confirms each element: (1) anthropic-wire/drain! (src/aux.anthropic.scm:933-939) calls anthropic-port->string before anthropic-wire/close! with no dynamic-wind, so a raise from read-string skips the close; (2) anthropic-send's condition-case (line 972-979) catches only (anthropic-error), so the raw raise escapes with the wire open, on both the 2xx path (anthropic/messages -> anthropic-wire->body! line 941-945) and the non-2xx drain inside anthropic-send (line 976); (3) the streaming path does wrap the fold in dynamic-wind with close in the after-thunk (lines 1656-1668), and the comment at 1620-1622 calls that dynamic-wind load bearing — exactly the discipline drain! lacks; (4) for the curl backend the skipped close thunk (lines 814-844) is what closes stdout/stderr, kills and reaps the child, so the leak is two fds plus a live/zombie child per failed request — and the claim's parenthetical is also right that the 0600 header file does NOT leak on this path, since it is deleted at first response-header block (line 867) before the wire exists; (5) the proposed fix is sound because anthropic-wire/close! is memoised-idempotent (lines 595-610).

#### [MINOR] `aux.anthropic.scm:1384` — Transient transport failure while reading a 2xx body is never retried

anthropic-send's retry loop (lines 964-987) only covers the transport call and the drain of NON-2xx wires: on a 2xx status it returns the wire immediately, and the body is drained later by anthropic-wire->body! at anthropic/messages line 1384, outside the condition-case. If the connection dies mid-body -- curl exit 18, 'transfer ended prematurely', which the module's own anthropic-transport/transient-codes table lists as retryable, and whose condition is duly marked retryable -- the anthropic-transport-error escapes with the whole retry budget unused. The identical exit code surfacing on a 5xx wire IS retried (asymmetry demonstrated below). Since the module already accepts re-POSTing on 5xx/429, re-POSTing on a truncated 2xx body is no less safe. Fix: on the ok-status branch inside anthropic-send's condition-case, drain the body there ((list 'ok w (anthropic-wire/drain! w)) or equivalent) so a transport error raised by the drain is caught by the same retry logic, and have anthropic/messages consume the already-drained text. The streaming path is deliberately unretried and needs no change.

**Evidence.** Custom transport returning status 200, a truncated body, and a close thunk answering (values 18 "curl: (18) transfer closed..."), with anthropic/retries=3 and a no-op sleep: anthropic/messages raises anthropic-transport-error with retryable? #t after the transport was called exactly 1 time. Contrast transport returning status 500 with the same exit-18 close: transport called 4 times (initial + 3 retries) before the error surfaces. Script: scratchpad/retry-gap.scm; output: 'transport error; retryable? #t; transport called 1 time(s) with a retry budget of 3' / '500-path transport error; transport called 4 time(s)'.

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.anthropic.scm: anthropic-send (lines 964-987) wraps in condition-case only the transport call and the drain of non-2xx wires; on ok status it returns `(list 'ok w)` undrained, and anthropic/messages drains at line 1384 via anthropic-wire->body!, outside any retry logic. anthropic-wire/drain! (933-939) raises anthropic-transport-error at close on nonzero exit, and exit 18 is in anthropic-transport/transient-codes (line 617), so the condition is marked retryable? #t (line 175) yet never retried on this path. Reproduced with scratchpad/retry-gap-verify.scm under /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi against the compiled scratch repo, custom transports whose close answers (values 18 "curl: (18) transfer closed..."), anthropic/retries=3, no-op sleep. Output: "2xx path: anthropic-transport-error; retryable? #t; transport called 1 time(s), budget was 3" vs "5xx path: anthropic-transport-error; retryable? #t; transport called 4 time(s), budget was 3" — the claimed asymmetry exactly. A third script (scratchpad/connect-retry.scm) confirmed connect-time retryable transport errors (exit 7 raised inside the transport) ARE retried and succeed on attempt 3, so the 2xx-drain path is the only place a retryable-marked transport error escapes with the budget unused.

#### [MINOR] `aux.anthropic.scm:1747` — anthropic/converse breaks on a vector of tools that anthropic/messages accepts

anthropic-request/body (line 1101) explicitly normalizes a VECTOR of tools with the comment 'a vector of tools must NOT be silently dropped: normalize before testing', and anthropic-tools->json (line 1217) does the same, so (anthropic/messages ... tools: (vector t)) works and sends the tools. anthropic/converse misses both spots: (anthropic-tools->table tools) at line 1747 does (for-each ... tools) which requires a list, so a vector dies with the cryptic '(for-each) bad argument type - not a list'; and even if the table accepted it, line 1758 'tools: (if (pair? tools) tools #f)' would silently pass #f, sending the request with no tools at all -- the exact silent drop the request-body comment guards against. Fix: normalize once at the top of anthropic/converse (let1 (tools (if (vector? tools) (vector->list tools) tools)) ...) or make anthropic-tools->table accept what anthropic-tools->json accepts.

**Evidence.** Repro (scratchpad/probe1-vector-tools.scm) against the stub transport: (anthropic/messages ... tools: (vector probe_echo/tool)) succeeds and the recorded request has a tools key; (anthropic/converse (list (anthropic-message/user "go")) tools: (vector probe_echo/tool)) raises 'bad argument type - not a list / args (#(#<aux.anthropic#anthropic-tool>))'.

**Independent verification.** Read src/aux.anthropic.scm: anthropic-request/body (~line 1101) and anthropic-tools->json (~1217) both normalize a vector of tools (comment: "a vector of tools must NOT be silently dropped"), but anthropic-tools->table (~1219) does (for-each ... tools) with no normalization, and anthropic/converse (~1758) passes tools: (if (pair? tools) tools #f). Reproduced with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi -q -s scratchpad/probe1-vector-tools.scm against the stub transport; output: "messages with vector tools ok, tools sent: #t" then "converse with vector tools RAISED: bad argument type - not a list / args (#(#<aux.anthropic#anthropic-tool>))". So the identical tools: (vector t) argument works in anthropic/messages and crashes in anthropic/converse.

### aux.continuation.delimited.scm

#### [CRITICAL] `aux.continuation.delimited.scm:30` — Second resume of a shift continuation across dynamic-wind/parameterize segfaults

Any `dynamic-wind` (or `parameterize`, which CHICKEN builds on the wind machinery) sitting between a `resetcc` and a `letcc/shift` capture point makes the captured continuation single-shot: the first resume works, the SECOND resume crashes the whole process with a segmentation violation. Root cause: `letcc` (aux.continuation.scm lines 11-14) is built on `continuation-capture`/`continuation-graft`, which do not save/reinstate CHICKEN's `##sys#dynamic-winds` list; re-grafting into the wind extent a second time leaves the runtime's wind bookkeeping pointing into a dead stack, and the next parameter lookup dereferences garbage. This is not a theoretical corner: every multi-branch probabilistic choice in (aux hansei) resumes its shift continuation once per branch (`probcc-distribution`, aux.hansei.scm line 79), and (aux nondeterministic)'s `chooseD` re-enters its `letcc` continuation once per choice, so `(probcc-reify/exact (parameterize ((q 'inner)) (probcc-coin 0.5)))` and `(letnondeterministic (? ¿ ⊦ • !) (parameterize ((q 'inner)) (? '(1 2 3))))` both reliably segfault in fully compiled -O2 code. hansei's own knobs (`op/times` etc.) are parameters, actively inviting `parameterize` in models. Additionally, in interpreted code (csi) even a SINGLE letcc escape from inside a plain `dynamic-wind` dies with 'call of non-procedure: #<unbound value>' (reproduced with bare `continuation-capture`/`continuation-graft`, so the root cause is in this CHICKEN 6.0.1pre1 build's core, but every operator this library layers on top — letcc, resetcc/shift, yield, hansei, amb, letnondeterministic — inherits it). Fix direction: have `letcc`'s hop capture the current `##sys#dynamic-winds` at capture time and reinstate it before grafting (mirroring what `##sys#call-with-current-continuation` does), or at minimum document loudly that no winder may separate a reset from its shifts and guard with a runtime check, because today the failure mode is memory-unsafe rather than an error.

**Evidence.** Minimal compiled repro (csc -O2): (import (scheme base) (aux base) (aux continuation) (aux continuation delimited)) (define q (make-parameter 'outer)) (define r (resetcc (parameterize ((q 'inner)) (let ((x (letcc/shift k k))) (list x (q)))))) (print (r 1)) (print (r 2)) => prints 'first resume: (1 inner)' then 'Error: segmentation violation' (exit 70) with call history ending at the `(q)` parameter read after continuation-graft. Same crash with plain dynamic-wind instead of parameterize (minwind6.scm), through hansei ((probcc-reify/exact (parameterize ((q 'inner)) (let ((x (probcc-coin 0.5))) (list x (q))))) => segfault in probcc-explore) and through letnondeterministic (nondet-param.scm => segfault). Interpreted single-shot repro: csi -e '(letcc k (dynamic-wind void (lambda () (k 1)) void))' => 'call of non-procedure: #<unbound value>'; identical crash with raw continuation-capture/continuation-graft, while the same code compiled returns 1 (skipping the after-thunk).

**Independent verification.** Reproduced all claimed failures with the exact binaries (/home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi and csc, CHICKEN_REPOSITORY_PATH set to the scratch auxrepo). (1) Minimal repro compiled with csc -O2: (resetcc (parameterize ((q 'inner)) (let ((x (letcc/shift k k))) (list x (q))))) printed "first: (1 inner)" then "Error: segmentation violation" (exit 70) on the second resume, call history ending at the (q) parameter read right after chicken.continuation#continuation-graft — byte-for-byte what the reviewer described. (2) Interpreted csi run of the same script also segfaults, and worse: the second resume first returned the WRONG value "(2 outer)" before crashing. (3) hansei repro (probcc-reify/exact (parameterize ((q 'inner)) ... (probcc-coin 0.5) ...)) compiled -O2: segfault, exit 70, history through aux.hansei.scm:79 k → continuation-graft → q. (4) letnondeterministic repro compiled -O2: segfault, exit 70, history through aux.nondeterministic.scm:37 continuation-graft → q. (5) Interpreted single-shot: csi -e '(letcc k (dynamic-wind void (lambda () (k 1)) void))' → "Error: call of non-procedure: #<unbound value>", exit 70, as claimed. (6) Control isolating root cause: the identical triple-resume-across-parameterize pattern using standard call-with-current-continuation instead of letcc runs correctly (got (0 inner)/(1 inner)/(2 inner), exit 0), proving CHICKEN's wind machinery works when winders are saved/reinstated and that the fault is letcc's continuation-capture/continuation-graft basis (aux.continuation.scm lines 11-14) skipping ##sys#dynamic-winds, exactly the claimed root cause. (7) Grepped README.md, AUX-CML.md, aux.continuation*.scm and tests: winder caveats are documented only for the unrelated (aux cml) module; nothing documents this restriction for resetcc/letcc/shift, so it is not intended behavior.

#### [MAJOR] `aux.continuation.delimited.scm:22` — Global *meta-continuation* leaks shift values into other srfi-18 threads' resets

`*meta-continuation*` is a single global variable, but srfi-18 threads share globals and this library ships its own threading layer (aux cml). If thread A is inside a `resetcc` and gets descheduled, thread B enters its own `resetcc` (installing B's meta-continuation), and A then performs `letcc/shift`'s abort (or its reset body returns), A's value is delivered to `*meta-continuation*` — which is now B's: A's shift result materializes as the result of B's unrelated reset, and B's own pending computation is abandoned. Verified: in the repro below thread B's reset evaluates to 'a-shifted, the value produced by thread A's shift, instead of its own 'b-normal. Any program combining (aux cml)/srfi-18 threads with resetcc/shift, yield, letnondeterministic or hansei in more than one thread can silently swap results between threads. Fix: make the meta-continuation per-thread — e.g. store it in a hash table keyed by `(current-thread)` or in a thread-specific slot (a srfi-39 parameter is the natural fit but is currently ruled out by the dynamic-wind segfault finding), and have `delimcc-reset`/`delimcc-shift` read and write the current thread's slot.

**Evidence.** Compiled repro (csc -O2, mcthread2.scm): thread A runs (resetcc (begin (thread-sleep! 0.05) (letcc/shift k 'a-shifted))); thread B runs (resetcc (begin (thread-sleep! 0.2) 'b-normal)). Both started, both joined. Output: 'a=a-shifted expected a-shifted' and 'b=a-shifted expected b-normal' — B's reset returned A's shifted value. Sequencing where B's reset closes before A shifts (mcthread.scm) works, confirming the corruption occurs exactly when a shift fires while another thread's reset is the installed meta-continuation.

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.continuation.delimited.scm: *meta-continuation* (line 22) is a single module-global; delimcc-reset (24-28) installs its escape continuation into it and delimcc-shift (30-33) applies whatever it currently holds — no thread keying anywhere. Ran the claimed interleaving with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi against the pre-compiled -O2 modules (scratchpad/mcthread-verify.scm): thread A = (resetcc (begin (thread-sleep! 0.05) (letcc/shift k 'a-shifted))), thread B started 0.01s later = (resetcc (begin (thread-sleep! 0.2) 'b-normal)). Output: "a=a-shifted (expected a-shifted)" / "b=a-shifted (expected b-normal)" — B's reset returned A's shift value, exactly as the reviewer reported. Checked src/test/delimcc.scm and delimcont.scm and the README: all delimcc tests are single-threaded and no single-thread restriction is documented, while the library ships its own (aux cml) threading layer, so this is not intended behavior.

#### [MINOR] `aux.continuation.delimited.scm:24` — Exception through resetcc leaves *meta-continuation* pointing into the dead computation

delimcc-reset (lines 24-28) installs a *meta-continuation* that is only restored when the body finishes normally (the restore lives inside the installed lambda and in the normal-return path). If the thunk raises, the exception unwinds past resetcc but *meta-continuation* keeps pointing at the aborted reset's continuation. A later shift outside any reset then silently resumes the dead computation instead of hitting the 'Missing enclosing resetcc' warner: in the repro, the stray shift's value re-entered the already-finished (print "caught: " ...) call. Fix: run the thunk under handle-exceptions (or a condition handler) that restores the saved mc before re-signalling; dynamic-wind is unsuitable because reified subcontinuations legitimately re-enter.

**Evidence.** Repro run with csi: (handle-exceptions e 'caught (resetcc (say 'inside) (error "boom"))) prints 'caught: caught'; then a plain (print "stray shift result: " (letcc/shift k 'stray-value)) prints 'caught: stray-value' instead — the shift applied the stale meta-continuation, teleporting 'stray-value into the first resetcc's return point (the caught print re-ran) — and 'stray shift result:' never appears; no 'Missing enclosing resetcc' warning is emitted.

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.continuation.delimited.scm: delimcc-reset (lines 24-28) restores the saved mc only inside the installed *meta-continuation* lambda (line 27), which runs solely when the meta-continuation is invoked (normal return line 28, or a shift). An exception unwinding past resetcc invokes neither, so *meta-continuation* stays pointing at the dead reset's k. Ran the reviewer's repro with the specified csi (CHICKEN_REPOSITORY_PATH set to the scratch auxrepo, /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi -q -s .../scratchpad/delimcc-exn-repro.scm). Output was exactly: "inside" / "caught: caught" / "caught: stray-value" / "end of script reached". The stray (print "stray shift result: " (letcc/shift k 'stray-value)) never printed its own label; instead 'stray-value re-entered the first resetcc's already-completed (print "caught: " ...) continuation, and the 'Missing enclosing resetcc' warning (line 22) was never emitted. No test in src/test/delimcc.scm covers exceptions escaping resetcc, and no comment documents this as intended; the warner's existence shows stray shifts were meant to warn, which the stale mc silently defeats.

### chicken-simdjson.cpp

#### [CRITICAL] `chicken-simdjson.cpp:195` — Uncaught simdjson C++ exceptions abort the whole process

chicken_simdjson_parse_ondemand_callback (line 195) and chicken_simdjson_load_ondemand_callback (line 184) call padded_string::load / parser.iterate / the implicit document->value conversion with simdjson exceptions enabled and no try/catch. The exception propagates out of the extern "C" function into CHICKEN's C runtime frames, std::terminate fires, and the entire Scheme process dies with SIGABRT (core dump). This triggers not only on malformed input but on perfectly valid JSON: any top-level scalar document ("5", "true", "null", '"s"') raises SCALAR_DOCUMENT_AS_VALUE, a number like 1e999 raises NUMBER_ERROR, and a missing file raises IO_ERROR — each one kills the process instead of raising a Scheme error. Fix: wrap the bodies of both callbacks (and the visit function, or compile with SIMDJSON_EXCEPTIONS=0 and check error codes) in try/catch, return NULL plus an error message the Scheme wrapper turns into (error ...); handle ondemand::json_type::number/scalar documents at top level via document.is_scalar()/get_value().

**Evidence.** All reproduced with csi: (simdjson-parse/ondemand "{bad json") -> 'terminate called after throwing an instance of simdjson::simdjson_error ... Aborted (core dumped)', exit 134. Same for (simdjson-load/ondemand "/nonexistent/file.json") (IO_ERROR), (simdjson-parse/ondemand "[1e999]") (NUMBER_ERROR), and every top-level scalar: '5', '"s"', 'null', 'true' all abort with SCALAR_DOCUMENT_AS_VALUE while '[5]', '[]', '{}' work.

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/chicken-simdjson.cpp: chicken_simdjson_parse_ondemand_callback (line 195) and chicken_simdjson_load_ondemand_callback (line 184) call padded_string::load / parser.iterate / the implicit document->value conversion (via chicken_simdjson_visit_ondemand(ddoc)) with simdjson exceptions enabled and zero try/catch anywhere in the file; the Scheme wrapper aux.simdjson.scm calls them via plain foreign-lambda with no error path (a NULL return would just crash simdjson->scheme anyway). Reproduced every claimed case with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi against the pre-compiled scratch repo: (simdjson-parse/ondemand "{bad json") -> "terminate called after throwing an instance of 'simdjson::simdjson_error' what(): INCOMPLETE_ARRAY_OR_OBJECT ..."; (simdjson-load/ondemand "/nonexistent/file.json") -> IO_ERROR abort; (simdjson-parse/ondemand "[1e999]") -> NUMBER_ERROR abort; (simdjson-parse/ondemand "5") and "null" -> SCALAR_DOCUMENT_AS_VALUE abort; while "[5]" -> #(5) and "{}" -> () succeed. Exit code verified: 'Aborted (core dumped)', csi exit 134 (SIGABRT). Additionally verified the crash is uncatchable from Scheme: (handle-exceptions e (print "caught in scheme") (simdjson-parse/ondemand "{bad")) still aborts with exit 134 — the handler never runs. Tests in src/test/ (testsuite-auxtest.html) only exercise well-formed object/array documents (twitter.json), so no test documents error handling; SIGABRT on any bad or scalar input is clearly not intended semantics.

#### [CRITICAL] `chicken-simdjson.cpp:201` — Top-level scalar JSON document aborts the whole process

chicken_simdjson_parse_ondemand_callback and chicken_simdjson_load_ondemand_callback hand the ondemand::document to chicken_simdjson_visit_ondemand(ondemand::value): the implicit document-to-value conversion calls get_value(), which throws SCALAR_DOCUMENT_AS_VALUE for any document whose root is a scalar. So every VALID top-level scalar JSON document — "5", "true", "null", "\"hello\"" — terminates the entire CHICKEN process with SIGABRT (exit 134). This is distinct from the already-reported uncaught-exception-on-invalid-input abort: these inputs are valid RFC 8259 JSON and the fix is different — branch on ddoc.is_scalar() (or check the error code of ddoc.get_value()) and handle scalar roots via the document's own get_int64()/get_bool()/get_string()/is_null() before recursing, in both entry points (lines 190 and 201).

**Evidence.** csi -e '(import (aux simdjson)) (print (simdjson-parse/ondemand "5"))' => terminate called after throwing an instance of 'simdjson::simdjson_error'  what(): SCALAR_DOCUMENT_AS_VALUE ... Aborted (core dumped), exit 134. Identical aborts reproduced for "\"hello\"", "true" and "null". Compound documents ({"a":[1,2,3]}) parse fine, exit 0.

**Independent verification.** Reproduced live: CHICKEN_REPOSITORY_PATH=<scratch auxrepo> /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi -q -e '(import (aux simdjson)) (print (simdjson-parse/ondemand "5"))' => "terminate called after throwing an instance of 'simdjson::simdjson_error'  what(): SCALAR_DOCUMENT_AS_VALUE ... Aborted (core dumped)", exit 134. Same abort for "true", "null", "\"hello\""; {"a":[1,2,3]} parses fine (exit 0). Code confirms mechanism: chicken-simdjson.cpp line 11 declares chicken_simdjson_visit_ondemand(ondemand::value), lines 190 and 201 pass the ondemand::document ddoc, forcing the implicit document->value conversion (get_value()) that throws SCALAR_DOCUMENT_AS_VALUE for scalar roots; grep shows no try/catch anywhere in the file, so the exception escapes and std::terminate kills the process.

### aux.unittest.scm

#### [MAJOR] `aux.unittest.scm:33` — Raising setup or teardown aborts the whole suite run uncaught

In unittest/testcase-run the setup call (line 33, inside the let-values init) and the teardown call (line 67) both run OUTSIDE the condition-case that guards the test body (lines 38-62). If a suite's setup or teardown raises, the condition escapes unittest/testcase-run, unittest/testsuite-run and unittest/✓ entirely: no failure is recorded in the result, the remaining test cases never run, no summary is printed and no HTML report is written. For a test framework this is the worst failure mode — one broken fixture silently kills the entire run instead of being reported as N failures. Fix: wrap the setup call and the teardown call in their own condition-case (or move them inside the existing one), recording a failure against the testcase name and continuing with the next case.

**Evidence.** (define-suite crashing-setup-suite ((setup t) (error "setup exploded")) ((test-one t) (⊦= 1 1))) then (unittest/✓ crashing-setup-suite) => the error 'setup exploded' propagates out of unittest/✓; no result summary, no report file. Same for a suite whose teardown raises: the test itself passed but 'teardown exploded' escapes and the passing result is lost. Reproduced with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi against the compiled scratch repo (script: /data/bitcoin/sandbox/claude-1005/-data-bitcoin-mnocentini-home-Developer-working-copies-aux-scm/9122609b-5bd5-4b7b-bf1d-9789f3aebddf/scratchpad/round2-unittest.scm).

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.unittest.scm: the setup call sits in the let-values init at line 33 and the teardown call at line 67, both outside the condition-case that spans lines 38-62, so nothing in unittest/testcase-run, unittest/testsuite-run (a plain map, line 127-130) or unittest/✓ (line 132-154) catches a condition raised by either fixture — the SXML->HTML->file! and pretty-print of the summary at lines 146-153 are never reached. Reproduced with the compiled scratch repo: (1) round2-unittest.scm run under /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi prints "setup-raises => EXN: setup exploded" and "teardown-raises => EXN: teardown exploded" — both conditions escape unittest/✓ and no summary is printed; the teardown case loses an already-passing test result. (2) A sharper repro (scratchpad/verify-setup-abort.scm) with a two-case suite whose setup raises only for the first case prints: "ESCAPED: setup exploded", "test-two ran? #f", "report file exists? #f" — confirming the remaining test cases never run, no failure is recorded, and no HTML report is written. src/test/bootstrap.scm only exercises non-raising setup/teardown, and no comment marks the escape as intended.

#### [MAJOR] `aux.unittest.scm:34` — Duplicate case name in define-suite silently runs first body twice

define-suite builds an alist ((casename f code) ...) and unittest/testcase-run fetches the procedure with (alist-ref testcase-name methods), which always returns the FIRST entry for that name. With two cases sharing a name (an easy copy-paste slip), the suite creates two testcases, runs the first body twice, and never executes the second body at all — its assertions can be arbitrarily false and the summary still shows all green with the full 'ran' count, so nothing looks off. Fix: have define-suite (or unittest/✓) check the case-name list with pairwise-different? / a duplicate scan and raise at suite-construction time, or carry the (f code) pair inside the testcase record instead of re-looking it up by name.

**Evidence.** Repro (scratchpad r3-dup.scm): (define-suite dup-suite ((t1 _) (⊦= 1 1)) ((t1 _) (⊦= 1 2))) (unittest/✓ dup-suite) prints ((ran 2) (failed 0)) — the (⊦= 1 2) assertion, which must fail if executed, never runs.

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.unittest.scm: define-suite (line 83-87) builds an alist with one (casename f code) entry per case; unittest/✓ (line 132-142) creates one testcase per entry (so ran counts both), but unittest/testcase-run line 34 re-fetches the procedure by name with (alist-ref testcase-name methods), which returns the first matching entry only. Ran scratchpad/dup-verify.scm with the specified csi (/home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi, CHICKEN_REPOSITORY_PATH set to the scratch auxrepo): (define-suite dup-suite ((t1 _) (print "FIRST BODY RAN") (⊦= 1 1)) ((t1 _) (print "SECOND BODY RAN") (⊦= 1 2))) printed ((ran 2) (failed 0)); a control suite with distinct names t1/t2 and the same failing assertion printed ((ran 2) (failed 1 (t2 (expected 1) (got 2)))). The generated testsuite-dup-suite.html contains "FIRST BODY RAN" in both testcases' stdout captures and zero occurrences of "SECOND BODY RAN", proving the first body ran twice and the second (failing) body never executed while the summary stayed all green.

#### [MAJOR] `aux.unittest.scm:132` — Red suite never fails the process: unittest/✓ result discarded, exit code 0

unittest/✓ returns the unittest/result record, but every one of the ~40 test scripts under src/test/ calls (unittest/✓ suite) at top level and discards it, and nothing in the framework touches the process exit status. A suite with failing tests prints (failed 1 ...) yet the csi process exits 0, so make test / any CI wrapper reports success no matter how many assertions fail. The failure is only visible to a human reading stdout or the generated HTML. Fix at the framework boundary: give unittest/✓ (or a variant, e.g. unittest/✓! used by the scripts) a final step that calls (exit 1) — or records into a global that a script epilogue checks — when (unittest/result-failed r) is non-null. As it stands the whole test infrastructure can go red without any automated signal.

**Evidence.** Repro (scratchpad r3-unittest.scm): (define-suite failing-suite ((ok _) (⊦= 1 1)) ((broken _) (⊦= 1 2))) (unittest/✓ failing-suite). Output: ((ran 2) (failed 1 (broken (expected 1) (got 2)))) then 'reached end of script normally', and echo $? prints EXIT CODE: 0. grep over src/test/*.scm shows only anthropic-live.scm even looks at the returned result, and it too never exits nonzero.

**Independent verification.** 1) Code read: /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.unittest.scm lines 132-154 — unittest/✓ builds the result record, writes the HTML report, pretty-prints the summary and returns r; nothing in the module ever calls exit or records failure globally. 2) Live repro (scratchpad verify-unittest-exit.scm, run with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi -s under the scratch CHICKEN_REPOSITORY_PATH): output was "((ran 2) (failed 1 (broken (expected 1) (got 2))))" followed by "EXIT CODE: 0". 3) make test (src/Makefile `test:` target) runs each suite as `csi -s <file>.scm`, so make only stops on a nonzero exit — which red suites never produce. 4) The repo itself documents the hazard: src/test/anthropic.scm lines 2081-2087 say verbatim "unittest/✓ never exits non-zero (it returns the result record at aux.unittest.scm:154 and nothing inspects it), so a completely broken module would still make `make test` -- and therefore `docker build` -- green", and that file opts out locally with an (exit 1) epilogue.

#### [MINOR] `aux.unittest.scm:53` — Failing test's captured stdout/stderr reported as empty strings

In unittest/testcase-run, every condition-case handler returns (list witness no-outsrt no-outsrt) (lines 53, 59, 62), so the per-test HTML report renders (stdout "") (stderr "") (and eta 0) for any failing test — exactly the case where the diagnostic output the test printed before failing would be most useful. The output is genuinely lost, not just dropped: with-output-to-string never returns when the body raises, so the set! of outstr never happens. Fix: allocate the two string ports outside the condition-case (open-output-string), run the body under with-output-to-port / with-error-output-to-port, and read (get-output-string port) in both the success path and the handlers — then a failing test's report shows what it printed up to the raise.

**Evidence.** csi repro with (define-suite printing-suite ((test-fails t) (print "diagnostic output before failing") (⊦= 1 2)) ...): unittest/testcase-run for test-fails returns sxml containing (stdout "") (stderr "") (eta 0.0), while the identical passing test's sxml contains (stdout "diagnostic output before passing\n"). Script: scratchpad/round2-stdout.scm.

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.unittest.scm: all three condition-case handlers (lines 53, 59, 62) return (list witness no-outsrt no-outsrt) with no-outsrt bound to "" (line 36), and eta-time stays 0 because the set! at line 47 is inside the raising body; with-output-to-string (line 42) never returns on a raise so the captured text is unreachable. Reproduced with csi: script scratchpad/verify-stdout-loss.scm defines a suite where test-fails does (print "diagnostic output before failing") then (⊦= 1 2), and test-passes prints then (⊦= 1 1). Output: failing test sxml = ((eta 0.0) ... (stdout "") (stderr "")); passing test sxml = (stdout "diagnostic output before passing\n"). The failing test's print never reached the terminal either, so the output is genuinely lost. No test or comment indicates this is intended; the capture machinery exists precisely to record per-test output.

#### [MINOR] `aux.unittest.scm:110` — ⊦= on two identical infinities always raises: inf-inf is NaN

equal-approx?'s real branch (line 110) computes (< (abs (- a b)) p). For a = b = +inf.0 (or -inf.0), (- a b) is +nan.0, and (< +nan.0 p) is #f, so (⊦= +inf.0 +inf.0) signals an assertion failure even though the two values are identical — and (equal=? +inf.0 +inf.0) is #t, so the non-real fallback would have passed them. Any test asserting an infinite expected value (e.g. the documented empty-list results of foldr/max => -inf.0, foldr/min => +inf.0, or the exported ∞ / -∞ constants of aux.base) can never pass. Fix: ((and (real? a) (real? b)) (or (= a b) (< (abs (- a b)) p))) — the (= a b) short-circuit handles equal infinities (and is also marginally faster for the common exact-equality case) while leaving NaN ≠ NaN behavior intact.

**Evidence.** csi with compiled modules: (⊦= +inf.0 +inf.0) => raises assert-equal; (⊦= -inf.0 -inf.0) => raises; (⊦= -inf.0 (foldr/max '())) => raises; meanwhile (equal=? +inf.0 +inf.0) => #t. Observed output: inf=inf => RAISED, -inf=-inf => RAISED, inf-via-max => RAISED.

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.unittest.scm lines 107-119: equal-approx?'s real branch is exactly ((and (real? a) (real? b)) (< (abs (- a b)) p)), and ⊦= uses (equal-approx? 0.000001). Ran the claimed repro with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi against the compiled scratch repo:
  inf=inf => RAISED
  -inf=-inf => RAISED
  equal=? +inf +inf: #t (so the non-real fallback would have passed them)
  1=1 => OK, 1.0=1.0 => OK
  foldr/max empty: -inf.0; inf-via-max ((⊦= -inf.0 (foldr/max (list)))) => RAISED
  (- +inf.0 +inf.0) = +nan.0 (mechanism confirmed)
  nan=nan => RAISED (unchanged by the proposed fix, since (= +nan.0 +nan.0) is #f)

#### [MINOR] `aux.unittest.scm:111` — equal-approx? recurses into pairs but not vectors

equal-approx? applies the epsilon comparison to reals and recurses through pairs, but any other compound (vectors, records) falls to equal=?, which compares numbers with exact =. So ⊦= on structurally identical data behaves inconsistently depending on the container: floats inside a list compare with tolerance 1e-6, the same floats inside a vector demand exact equality and fail spuriously. Fix: add a vector clause mirroring the pair clause, e.g. ((and (vector? a) (vector? b)) (and (= (vector-length a) (vector-length b)) (every-index ? a b))).

**Evidence.** csi: (⊦= (list 1.0000000001) (list 1)) → passes; (⊦= (vector 1.0000000001) (vector 1)) → signals assert-equal failure. Both hold the same numbers within the 1e-6 tolerance.

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.unittest.scm lines 107-117: equal-approx? has clauses for (real? a)/(real? b) and (pair? a)/(pair? b) only; everything else falls to equal=?, which compares numbers with = (no tolerance). Ran against the compiled (aux unittest) with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi: (⊦= (list 1.0000000001) (list 1)) → "list -> passed"; (⊦= (vector 1.0000000001) (vector 1)) → "vector -> FAILED: ((exn message assert-equal failed) (unittest-assert-equal comparison ((expected #(1.0000000001)) (got #(1)))))"; scalar case passed. This is exactly the reviewer's claimed inconsistency.

#### [MINOR] `aux.unittest.scm:115` — ⊦ failure path breaks for non-2-arity predicates

(⊦ pred? . args) advertises variadic predicates, but on failure it does (apply unittest/condition-expected-actual args) and that constructor takes exactly (a b). Any failing assertion with one argument, e.g. (⊦ odd? 4), or more than two, e.g. (⊦ < 1 2 3 1), crashes with 'bad argument count' instead of signalling the (exn unittest-assert-equal) condition. Inside a suite the generic (c (exn)) handler still records a failure, but the report shows an arity error in the framework instead of the expected/got comparison, and the assertion is unusable outside a suite. Fix: make unittest/condition-expected-actual variadic, e.g. (define (unittest/condition-expected-actual . args) (condition '(exn message "assert failed") `(unittest-assert-equal comparison ((values ,args))))) or pattern the 2-arg case specially.

**Evidence.** csi -q -e '(import (aux unittest)) (⊦ odd? 4)' → Error: bad argument count - received 1 but expected 2: #<procedure (aux.unittest#unittest/condition-expected-actual a b)>. Same with (⊦ < 1 2 3 1): received 4 but expected 2.

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.unittest.scm: line 115 defines variadic (⊦ pred? . args) which on failure does (apply unittest/condition-expected-actual args); line 156 defines (unittest/condition-expected-actual a b) with fixed 2-arity. Reproduced with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi and the scratch CHICKEN_REPOSITORY_PATH: (⊦ odd? 4) → "Error: bad argument count - received 1 but expected 2: #<procedure (aux.unittest#unittest/condition-expected-actual a b)>"; (⊦ < 1 2 3 1) → same error, "received 4 but expected 2"; control (⊦ = 1 2) correctly signals "Error: assert-equal failed". Non-2-arity use is intended, not hypothetical: src/test/dmatch.scm:75 has (⊦ void? (dint (display 'x))), a 1-argument predicate that works only because it never fails. The suite runner's generic (c (exn)) clause (aux.unittest.scm lines 55-59) still records a failure inside a suite, so no test silently passes.

#### [MINOR] `aux.unittest.scm:160` — ⊦⧳ passes silently when body raises nothing

⊦⧳ is condition-case with handler clauses only: when the body completes without raising, the form returns the body's value and no failure is recorded. Several test call sites use it as an assert-raises (test/fds.queue.scm:55-56 on empty-queue accessors, test/dmatch.scm:20,44,77 on must-fail matches), so if the code under test ever stops raising, those tests keep passing silently — exactly the regression an assert-raises exists to catch. The repo already knows this: test/anthropic.scm:8 notes '⊦⧳ TOLERATES a condition rather than requiring one' and test/cml-lib.scm:24 defines a local ⊦raises workaround: (define-syntax-rule (⊦raises (kind ...) body ...) (⊦= 'raised (condition-case (begin body ... 'not-raised) ((kind ...) 'raised)))). Fix: promote that ⊦raises into (aux unittest) and migrate the must-raise call sites; keep ⊦⧳ only where tolerate semantics is genuinely wanted.

**Evidence.** csi -q -e '(import (aux unittest)) (print "no-raise result: " (⊦⧳ ((exn)) 42)) (print "silently passed")' printed: no-raise result: 42 / silently passed — no condition signaled, so inside a suite the test is marked pass.

**Independent verification.** Read aux.unittest.scm:160-165: ⊦⧳ is (condition-case (begin body ...) ((exn ...) (void)) ...) with no no-raise detection, exactly as claimed. Reproduced with the pre-compiled module: CHICKEN_REPOSITORY_PATH=<scratch auxrepo> /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi -q -e '(import (aux unittest)) (print "no-raise result: " (⊦⧳ ((exn)) 42)) (print "silently passed")' printed "no-raise result: 42" then "silently passed" — no condition, so inside a suite this records a pass. Verified the cited call sites: test/fds.queue.scm:53-56 (test-empty-car's ONLY accessor assertions are the two ⊦⧳ forms; csi check confirmed fds-queue-car on an empty queue currently raises (exn), so a regression removing that raise would leave the test green and empty) and test/dmatch.scm:20,44,77 (line 77 comments "; error: unbound x" — must-raise intent). Also verified the local strict workarounds exist as claimed: test/cml-lib.scm:23-25 defines ⊦raises verbatim as quoted, and anthropic.scm:8, anthropic-live.scm:27, stream.sicp.scm:5, fds.unionfind.scm:160, cml.scm:26, cml-io.scm:29 all document avoiding ⊦⧳ for negative paths.

### aux.category.monad.scm

#### [MAJOR] `aux.category.monad.scm:35` — do/monad ← pattern-match failure raises instead of calling M:fail

The destructuring bind clause ((← (pat ...) expr) ...) expands to (▷ expr (λ1-match/first ((pat ...) ...))), and λ1-match/first with a single clause raises 'match/first: uncaught value' on any element that does not match. The module's own header documents the Haskell MonadFail contract (fail msg = error msg overridable per instance), and every instance that can benefit defines fail accordingly (list/maybe/prob/ziplist fail = '()), yet do/monad never routes pattern failure to it — fail is only reachable from the degenerate (do/monad) form. So the canonical list-monad idiom 'bind a pattern to filter out non-matching shapes' crashes the whole computation instead of skipping the element. Fix: add an else clause in the expansion: (λ1-match/first ((pat ...) (do/monad body ...)) (else (M:fail 'pattern-mismatch))). This may be a deliberate strictness choice, but it contradicts the documented model and makes the instances' fail definitions dead code in practice.

**Evidence.** (do/monad (← (,x . ,xs) '((1 2) () (3 4))) ,(cons x xs)) with the list monad raises 'match/first: uncaught value' when it reaches '(), instead of producing ((1 2) (3 4)) via fail='(). Call history shows the first element bound fine ((M:return (cons x xs))) before the fk error thunk fired on '().

**Independent verification.** Code reading: /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.category.monad.scm line 35 expands (← (pat ...) expr) to (▷ expr (λ1-match/first ((pat ...) (do/monad body ...)))); aux.base.scm lines 83-130 show λ1-match/first is match/first with an implicit else that calls (error "match/first: uncaught value...") (aux.base.scm:88). M:fail appears in exactly one expansion clause, the empty (do/monad) form (line 44). Repro (scratchpad/repro-monad-fail.scm, run with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi -q -s against the pre-built scratch repo): (do/monad (← (,x . ,xs) '((1 2))) ,(cons x xs)) prints ((1 2)) fine, but (do/monad (← (,x . ,xs) '((1 2) () (3 4))) ,(cons x xs)) with (aux category monad list) raises "Error: match/first: uncaught value. ()" and exits 70 — the call history shows (M:return (cons x xs)) succeeding for the first element before the fk error thunk fires on '(), exactly matching the reviewer's account. Instance survey: fail is required by the functor signature (M (return >>= fail)) and list/maybe/prob/ziplist all define (fail . args) => '() (difflist an empty difflist), yet the only route to it is the degenerate empty do form (tested at src/test/category-list.scm:16, ⊦= '() (do/monad)). No test exercises a refutable ← pattern.

#### [MAJOR] `aux.category.monad.scm:38` — do/monad destructuring let clause calls match/first with wrong shape

The clause ((do/monad (let (p ...) expr) body body* ...) (match/first (((p ...) expr) (do/monad body body* ...)))) passes the WHOLE form (((p ...) expr) (do/monad ...)) as the single scrutinee-expression argument of match/first, with no clauses. The scrutinee is therefore evaluated as code, i.e. ((pattern expr) (do/monad ...)) gets applied as a procedure call, and the pattern's unquotes leak into evaluation. Every use of the pattern-destructuring let form of do/monad crashes at runtime, for every monad instance. The argument shape is exactly that of match1/first ((pat v) body ...), so the fix is either (match1/first ((p ...) expr) (do/monad body body* ...)) or equivalently (match/first expr ((p ...) (do/monad body body* ...))). Note the sibling arrow form (← (pat ...) expr) at line 35 is correct (uses λ1-match/first), which is the workaround users are silently forced into.

**Evidence.** (import (aux base) (aux category list) (aux category monad list)) (do/monad (let (,a ,b) (list 1 2)) ,(+ a b)) => Error: unbound variable: unquote; call trace shows <eval> ((((unquote a) unquote b) (list 1 2)) (do/monad408 ...)) being applied. Same failure for (let (,a . ,b) (cons 1 2)). Non-pattern (let x 5) works fine, and the ← pattern form (do/monad (← (,a ,b) (list (list 1 2))) ,(+ a b)) => (3) works.

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.category.monad.scm line 38: ((do/monad (let (p ...) expr) body body* ...) (match/first (((p ...) expr) (do/monad body body* ...)))). Checked /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.base.scm line 83: match/first's shape is (match/first exp clause ...), so line 38 passes the entire ((pattern expr) (do/monad ...)) form as the scrutinee expression with zero clauses, and it is evaluated as a procedure application. Reproduced with the specified csi (/home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi against the scratch repo): script importing (aux base) (aux category list) (aux category monad list) gives: (do/monad (let x 5) ,(+ x 1)) => (6) [control ok]; (do/monad (← (,a ,b) (list (list 1 2))) ,(+ a b)) => (3) [control ok]; (do/monad (let (,a ,b) (list 1 2)) ,(+ a b)) => "Error: unbound variable: unquote", with call trace showing exactly the predicted mis-expansion being applied: ((((unquote a) (unquote b)) (list 1 2)) (do/monad504 (unquote (+ a b)))). Also verified both proposed fix shapes evaluate correctly: (match/first (list 1 2) ((,a ,b) (+ a b))) => 3 and (match1/first ((,a ,b) (list 1 2)) (+ a b)) => 3. grep over src/test/ shows no existing test exercises the let-pattern form (tests only use (let y x) variable form), which is why it went unnoticed.

#### [MAJOR] `aux.category.monad.scm:70` — ◇ passes reversed list to o/monad instead of applying it

(define (◇ . f*) (o/monad (reverse f*))) — o/monad takes rest arguments, so (o/monad (reverse f*)) makes f* inside o/monad a one-element list whose sole element is the list of functions. foldr then tries to apply that list as a Kleisli arrow, so every call of ◇ with one or more functions crashes when the resulting arrow is invoked (zero-arg ◇ also yields a broken arrow). Fix: (define (◇ . f*) (apply o/monad (reverse f*))). This is exported by the (aux category monad) functor to every monad instance.

**Evidence.** (import (aux category list) (aux category monad list)) with kf = (λ (x) (list x (* 10 x))), kg = (λ (x) (list (+ x 1))): ((o/monad kg kf) 3) => (4 31) correct, but ((◇ kf kg) 3) => Error: call of non-procedure: (#<procedure (kg x)> #<procedure (kf x)>) — the reversed argument list itself is being applied.

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.category.monad.scm line 69-70: (define (o/monad . f*) ...) takes rest args; (define (◇ . f*) (o/monad (reverse f*))) passes the reversed list as ONE argument, so inside o/monad f* = ((list-of-arrows)) and foldr applies that list as a procedure. Ran a repro in the scratchpad (CHICKEN_REPOSITORY_PATH set to the scratch auxrepo, /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi -q -s diamond-test.scm) importing (aux category list) then (aux category monad list), with kf = (λ (x) (list x (* 10 x))) and kg = (λ (x) (list (+ x 1))). Output: "o/monad: (4 31)" (correct) then "diamond crashed: call of non-procedure args: ((#<procedure (kg x)> #<procedure (kf x)>))" — exactly the reviewer's evidence. The fix (apply o/monad (reverse f*)) is correct. Also checked: no test in src/test/ exercises the monadic ◇ (the series:◇ hits are an unrelated definition in aux.stream.series.scm), and the same broken body is baked into aux.category.monad.import.scm, so every functor instance (list, maybe, prob, reader, writer, ziplist, difflist, identity, ...) exports the broken operator.

#### [MINOR] `aux.category.monad.scm:39` — do/monad let clause rejects the ,expr form: unbound variable unquote

The ← clauses normalize an unquoted right-hand side ((← var ,e) at line 36 and (← (pat ...) ,e) at line 34 rewrite ,e to (M:return e)), but the two let clauses (lines 38-39) have no such normalization. So (do/monad (let a ,expr) ...) expands to (let1 (a (unquote expr)) ...) and dies at runtime with 'unbound variable: unquote' (or a compile error in compiled code). The library's own documentation relies on this shape: the usage comment in aux.category.writer.scm lines 74-78 writes (do/monad (let a ,(make-category-difflist ...)) ...), which fails as written. Fix: add clauses ((do/monad (let var (unquote expr)) body ...) (do/monad (let var expr) body ...)) and the analogous one for the pattern-let form.

**Evidence.** Ran with csi: (do/monad (let a ,1) ,a) in the list monad => 'Error: unbound variable: unquote', call history shows (let1 (a (unquote 1)) ...); the sibling form (do/monad (← a ,1) ,a) works and returns (1).

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.category.monad.scm lines 32-44: unquote is a syntax-rules literal and the two ← clauses (lines 34, 36) normalize (unquote e) to (M:return e), but the two let clauses (lines 38-39) have no such clause, so in (do/monad (let var ,e) ...) the pattern variable expr captures (unquote e) whole and expands to (let1 (var (unquote e)) ...); let1 in aux.base.scm (lines 16-19) is a plain let wrapper that does not strip unquote. Reproduced with the pre-compiled scratch repo and /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi: (do/monad (← a ,1) ,a) => (1); (do/monad (let a 1) ,a) => (1); (do/monad (let a ,1) ,a) => "Error: unbound variable: unquote", with the call history showing the expansion (do/monad (let a (unquote 1)) (unquote a)) and (M:return435 a) reached only after the (unquote 1) binding fails. The pattern-let form (let (,x . ,y) ,expr) fails the same way. The failing shape is exactly what the library's own usage examples write: aux.category.writer.scm lines 75-77 and 94-96 use (let a ,(make-category-difflist ...)) / (let x ,1) — though those lines sit inside the block comment spanning lines 37-109, so they are never compiled, which is why the build and test suites (e.g. src/test/category-list.scm, which only uses (let y x) without unquote) never trip over it.

#### [MINOR] `aux.category.monad.scm:42` — do/monad with trailing binding clause dies with 'unbound variable: ←'

A do/monad whose last clause is a binder, e.g. (do/monad (← x (list 1 2))), matches none of the ← patterns (they all require at least one following body form) and falls through to the catch-all ((do/monad expr) expr), which emits (← x (list 1 2)) as an ordinary application of the unbound variable ←. The user gets 'Error: unbound variable: ←' with no mention of do/monad. Same for a trailing (let var expr) clause, which becomes a malformed named let. Adding explicit clauses ((do/monad (← . _)) (syntax-error "do/monad: binding clause needs a body")) and the analogous let clause before the catch-all turns a baffling runtime error into a precise expansion-time diagnostic.

**Evidence.** (import (aux category list) (aux category monad list)) (do/monad (← x (list 1 2))) => 'Error: unbound variable: ←', raised from the expanded application form, with a call history pointing into the import library rather than the user's code.

**Independent verification.** Read src/aux.category.monad.scm: all ← patterns (lines 34-37) and let patterns (38-39) require at least one body form; a trailing binder falls to the catch-all ((do/monad expr) expr) on line 42, emitting the clause verbatim. Reproduced with the pre-built scratch repo: csi -e '(import (aux category list) (aux category monad list)) (print (do/monad (← x (list 1 2))))' => "Error: unbound variable: ←" with call history pointing into aux.category.monad.list.import.scm expander internals, exactly as the reviewer described. Also (do/monad (let y 42)) => "Error: during expansion of (let ...) - not a proper list: (let y 42)" (expansion-time, but names let, not do/monad). Tests in src/test/category-list.scm never end a do/monad with a binder, so no intended behavior is contradicted by adding a syntax-error clause.

### aux.kanren.date.scm
