#### [MAJOR] `aux.kanren.date.scm:720` — weekday-midnight° answers midnights before lo when lo is mid-day

The doc (lines 707-719) promises 't ranges over the midnights UTC of the w-days inside the instant window [lo,hi]'. But weekday-midnight° converts lo to day-space with (day+second° lo dlo slo), which FLOORS: dlo is the day lo falls on, and day-of-week/within° then includes dlo itself. When lo is not exactly midnight and lo's own day is a w-day, the relation answers that day's midnight, an instant strictly BEFORE lo, i.e. outside the queried window. The hi edge is correct (flooring hi is the right direction there); only the lo edge over-approximates. Fix: anchor the day window at the first day whose midnight is >= lo, e.g. after (day+second° lo dlo slo) add (cond° ((=° slo '()) (=° dlo* dlo)) ((pos° slo) (plus° dlo '(1) dlo*))) and pass dlo* to day-of-week/within°. Note the whole day-of-week/within°/weekday-midnight°/month°/year° window layer has zero cases in test/microkanren-date.scm, so nothing pins this.

**Evidence.** With lo = (build-instant 2025 10 6 12 0 0) (Monday NOON) and hi = (build-instant 2025 10 31), (μkanren-run (q 10 #t) (fresh° (t) (weekday-midnight° 'monday lo hi t) (=° q t))) answered 2025-10-06T00:00:00Z, 2025-10-13T00:00:00Z, 2025-10-20T00:00:00Z, 2025-10-27T00:00:00Z — the first answer is 12 hours before lo (verified numerically: (numeral->number t) < (numeral->number lo)).

**Independent verification.** Reproduced with the specified csi. Script at scratchpad/wm-repro.scm: lo = (build-instant 2025 10 6 12 0 0) (Monday noon, 1759752000), hi = (build-instant 2025 10 31), query (μkanren-run (q 10 #t) (fresh° (t) (weekday-midnight° 'monday lo hi t) (=° q t))). Output: "1759708800 -> BEFORE lo (OUT OF WINDOW); 1760313600 -> in window; 1760918400 -> in window; 1761523200 -> in window". 1759708800 = 2025-10-06T00:00:00Z, 43200s before lo, violating the doc at src/aux.kanren.date.scm:707-708 ("midnights UTC of the w-days inside the instant window [lo,hi]"). Code reading confirms the mechanism: line 722 (day+second° lo dlo slo) floors lo to dlo; day-of-week/within° (line 577-587) strides from L + (mod (target - weekday-of-day L) 7), which is dlo itself when lo's day is a w-day, ignoring slo; midnight° then emits dlo*86400 < lo. The hi edge is correct (flooring hi is the right direction; stride/≤° bounds by dhi). Test coverage claim also verified: grep of src/test/microkanren-date.scm shows weekday-midnight° appears only in doc prose (lines 710, 1134), zero executable cases.

#### [MINOR] `aux.kanren.date.scm:191` — numeral->number accepts non-canonical numerals, guards add answers

numeral->number (lines 191-195) is documented as "the groundness test as well as the decoder ... groundness by positive typing", but it accepts any proper list of 0/1 cells, including non-canonical ones with a trailing 0: '(0) decodes to 0 and '(1 0) to 1. Relationally these terms are NOT numerals — (numeral° '(0)) and (numeral° '(1 0)) both fail, and (aux kanren arith) rejects them (e.g. (<° '(1 0) DAY) has zero answers). Consequently every project°-guarded fast path built on numeral->number/numerals->numbers (day+second°, civil°, clock°, day-of-week°, on-day°, iso°) treats a non-canonical term as a ground instant/day-count and SUCCEEDS where the pure fallback fails: (day+second° '(1 0) d sod) answers (d=() sod=(1)), yet the pure else-branch definition rejects exactly that triple (plus° '() '(1) '(1 0) has zero answers); (civil° '(0) y m d) answers 1970-01-01 just like day '(). This directly violates the module's central contract, stated at the seam: "The guard adds two fast paths over that definition and CHANGES NO ANSWER IN ANY MODE", and it also breaks the injectivity the arith module relies on ('(1) and '(1 0) become two terms denoting the same instant). Fix: make numeral->number return #f when the list is non-empty and its last cell is 0 (one extra check in the loop: reject a 0 cell whose cdr is null), so the decoder's domain coincides with the numeral° type.

**Evidence.** csi runs: (μkanren-run (q 1 #t) (numeral° '(0))) => () and (numeral° '(1 0)) => (), but (civil° '(0) y m d) => ((numeral-of-1970 (1) (1))) and (day+second° '(1 0) d sod) => ((() (1))). The pure check of that fast-path answer fails: (μkanren-run (q 1 #t) (fresh° (p) (<° '(1) DAY) (*° '() DAY p) (plus° p '(1) '(1 0)))) => () and (plus° '() '(1) '(1 0)) => (); the full pure else-branch query on t='(1 0) produced no answer in 60 s (its <° generator never matches), while the guarded relation answers instantly — the guard changes the answer set.

**Independent verification.** Read aux.kanren.date.scm lines 187-204 (numeral->number accepts any 0/1 list, no trailing-zero check) and 391-422 (seam contract "CHANGES NO ANSWER IN ANY MODE" plus the project° guard), and aux.kanren.arith.scm lines 330-345 (numeral° requires the last cell to be 1). Ran a repro script with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi against the scratch repository. Output: (numeral° '(0)) => (), (numeral° '(1 0)) => (), (plus° '() '(1) '(1 0)) => () [pure arith rejects], yet (day+second° '(1 0) d sod) => ((() (1))) — identical to the answer for canonical '(1) — and (civil° '(0) y m d) => same 1970-01-01 answer as (civil° '() y m d). The pure else-branch on the fast path's own answer, (fresh° (p) (<° '(1) DAY) (*° '() DAY p) (plus° p '(1) '(1 0))), => (), while the same query with t='(1) => (α). So the guard demonstrably admits answers the pure definition rejects, violating the module's stated invariant, and '(1) / '(1 0) denote the same instant, breaking injectivity.

#### [MINOR] `aux.kanren.date.scm:252` — instant->iso truncates years past 9999 to their last 4 digits

zero-pad is srfi-13 string-pad, which TRUNCATES ON THE LEFT when the string is longer than the width. So (zero-pad year 4) on a 5-digit year silently drops the leading digits: the instant of 12025-03-14T09:00:00Z formats as "2025-03-14T09:00:00Z" — a well-formed, wrong ISO string. The module's docs promise "a year past 9999 has no spelling here and the parser rejects anything not of that shape", but the FORMATTER neither rejects nor fails: iso° with t ground emits the corrupted string, and iso° with that string ground answers a different instant (1741942800 instead of 317311462800), so the relation is not symmetric and produces a silently wrong answer instead of zero answers. Year 10000 exactly becomes "0000-..." which at least fails to parse (pre-epoch). Fix: in instant->iso, raise when (> (car c) 9999) — matching number->numeral's stated philosophy that a wrong/hanging answer is worse than an error — or emit the unpadded year so the 21-char string at least fails closed in iso->instant.

**Evidence.** csi run: (instant->iso 317311462800) => "2025-03-14T09:00:00Z" (should be year 12025); (iso->instant that) => 1741942800, not equal to the original. Through the relation: (μkanren-run (q 1 #t) (iso° T q)) with T = numeral of 317311462800 gives "2025-03-14T09:00:00Z", and (iso° q str1) on that string gives the numeral of 1741942800. Year 10000: (instant->iso (* (days-from-civil 10000 1 1) 86400)) => "0000-01-01T00:00:00Z", which iso->instant rejects (#f), so t->str succeeds while str->t fails.

**Independent verification.** Reproduced with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi against the pre-compiled scratch repo: (instant->iso 317311462800) => "2025-03-14T09:00:00Z" (true year is 12025); (iso->instant "2025-03-14T09:00:00Z") => 1741942800, not the original instant, so the round trip silently answers a different instant. Also reproduced the year-10000 edge: (instant->iso (* (days-from-civil 10000 1 1) 86400)) => "0000-01-01T00:00:00Z", which iso->instant rejects (#f). Code at src/aux.kanren.date.scm:245 confirms zero-pad is srfi-13 string-pad, which keeps the right-hand end of a too-long string, and instant->iso (lines 247-255) does no range check.

#### [MINOR] `aux.kanren.date.scm:262` — iso->instant accepts malformed separators and signed fields

The module's documentation (lines 97-99 and 733-737) promises that the ISO parser 'rejects anything that is not exactly that shape' and that garbage strings give zero answers rather than a wrong instant. But `iso->instant` only checks the string length and the characters at positions 10 (#\T) and 19 (#\Z); the date separators at positions 4 and 7 (#\-) and the time separators at 13 and 16 (#\:) are never examined, and each field is decoded with `string->number`, which accepts a leading sign. So "2025x10-09T08:53:20Z" and "2025-10-09T08!53?20Z" both parse to 1760000000, and worse, "2025-+1-09T08:53:20Z" parses to 1736412800 (2025-01-09): a malformed string silently becomes a *different* instant, exactly the failure mode the validator claims to prevent. This propagates to the relation `iso°` (line 738), whose string-ground mode calls `iso->instant`. Fix: also require `(char=? #\- (string-ref s 4))`, `(char=? #\- (string-ref s 7))`, `(char=? #\: (string-ref s 13))`, `(char=? #\: (string-ref s 16))`, and check every field consists of digits only (or check `(>= y 0)` etc. plus reject any non-digit character) before calling `string->number`. Related asymmetry worth fixing in the same touch: `instant->iso` happily formats a year past 9999 into a 21-character string that `iso->instant` then rejects, so `iso°`'s two ground modes disagree at that margin, while the docs say such a year 'has no spelling here'.

**Evidence.** Ran with the compiled module: (map (lambda (s) (list s (iso->instant s))) '("2025x10-09T08:53:20Z" "2025-10-09T08!53?20Z" "2025-+1-09T08:53:20Z" "2025-10-09 08:53:20Z")) => (("2025x10-09T08:53:20Z" 1760000000) ("2025-10-09T08!53?20Z" 1760000000) ("2025-+1-09T08:53:20Z" 1736412800) ("2025-10-09 08:53:20Z" #f)). The genuine string "2025-10-09T08:53:20Z" also parses to 1760000000, so the malformed variants alias real instants; the '+1' month variant yields a different instant than its digits suggest.

**Independent verification.** Read src/aux.kanren.date.scm (~line 262): iso->instant checks only string length 20, #\T at index 10, #\Z at index 19; positions 4, 7 (#\-) and 13, 16 (#\:) are never examined, and fields go through string->number, which accepts a leading sign. Reproduced with the compiled module (csi at /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi, scratch CHICKEN_REPOSITORY_PATH): "2025-10-09T08:53:20Z" => 1760000000; "2025x10-09T08:53:20Z" => 1760000000; "2025-10-09T08!53?20Z" => 1760000000; "2025-+1-09T08:53:20Z" => 1736412800 (a DIFFERENT instant); "2025-10-09 08:53:20Z" => #f. This contradicts the module doc (lines 97-99: parser 'rejects anything that is not exactly that shape') and the iso° doc (lines 733-737: garbage gives 'ZERO answers rather than a wrong instant'), and the test suite's prose ('never a neighbouring instant'). test/microkanren-date.scm test/iso->instant/rejects covers 13 malformed strings, none with a bad interior separator or signed field, so the gap is untested rather than intended.

#### [MINOR] `aux.kanren.date.scm:545` — day-of-week° diverges on a ground non-weekday symbol with day fresh

day-of-week°'s fallback (line 545) generates the DAY-COUNT: (and° (numeral° day) (δ° (day-of-week° day w))). With day fresh and w ground to a symbol that is not one of the seven weekday names (a typo like 'mon or 'Monday), the (weekday-name? w*) branch is skipped, and the fallback enumerates every day-count forever, each re-entry failing the (=° w (weekday-of-day Z)) unification: no answer, no termination. The answer set projected on w is just seven symbols, so this is finitely refutable — and the module itself does it right one relation later: day-of-week/within°'s fallback (line 598) generates w with (weekday-name° w) first, with a comment explaining exactly this trap, so (day-of-week/within° lo hi 'mon d) fails finitely. Fix: make day-of-week°'s else branch (and° (weekday-name° w) (numeral° day) (δ° ...)) — a ground bogus w then fails in seven unifications, and with w fresh the neither-ground mode still enumerates (as seven interleaved strides via the weekday-name?-branch on re-entry). This also falsifies the doc claim in test/day-of-week° that the relation is 'TOTAL: no error, no hang'.

**Evidence.** (μkanren-run (d 5 #t) (day-of-week/within° (build-num 20366) (build-num 20372) 'mon d)) => () immediately, but (μkanren-run (d 1 #t) (day-of-week° d 'mon)) was killed by timeout after 20 s with no output (exit 124). Both-ground bogus w fails fine; only the day-fresh mode hangs.

**Independent verification.** Read aux.kanren.date.scm: day-of-week°'s else branch is (and° (numeral° day) (δ° (day-of-week° day w))) with no weekday-name° guard, while day-of-week/within°'s fallback generates w via weekday-name° with a comment saying generating day instead "LOOPS ... Measured, as a hang". Ran a repro script under the scratch repo with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi: (μkanren-run (d 5 #t) (day-of-week/within° (build-num 20366) (build-num 20372) 'mon d)) => () immediately; (μkanren-run (q 1 #t) (=° q 'yes) (day-of-week° (build-num 20366) 'mon)) => () immediately; (μkanren-run (d 3 #t) (day-of-week° d 'monday)) => ((0 0 1) (1 1 0 1) (0 1 0 0 1)) instantly; (μkanren-run (d 1 #t) (day-of-week° d 'mon)) produced no output and was killed by timeout 25 (exit 124). Test doc at src/test/microkanren-date.scm:765 claims the relation is "TOTAL: no error, no hang", which the repro falsifies for this mode.

### aux.cml.scm

#### [MAJOR] `aux.cml.scm:3009` — write-string-evt/output-evt initial readiness wait has no close watch: writer stranded forever after port close + fd reuse

The module's design notes promise that a writer waiting on a descriptor port that another thread closes is woken by the 0.1s close sweep, 'since the number of a descriptor that another thread closed may belong to another file by then, whose readiness says nothing about the port'. %port-output-wait (line 2928, used for chunks 2..n and the lock-busy case) does register a %close-watch!, and the reader helper's wait does too (line 2741). But the INITIAL commit wait of write-string-evt on a descriptor port -- (wrap (io-evt fd 'output) (lambda ignored (%port-write-chunks! port fd s #t))) at line 3009 -- and output-evt's wait at line 2908 are bare io-evt waits with no close watch. If the port is closed while the sync is blocked there and the fd number is reused by another open file that never becomes writable (a pipe's read end, an idle socket), the writer thread waits forever: no error, no wakeup, violating the documented guarantee that holds on every other port wait. (Without fd reuse the bug is masked because CHICKEN's file-select reports a closed fd as ready -- POLLNVAL -- and %port-write-chunks! then sees port-closed? and raises; the mid-write waits raise within ~100ms via the sweep, verified separately.) Fix: register the port with %close-watch! around the initial wait as well, e.g. build the fd path of output-evt/write-string-evt as a with-nack event whose commit waits on (choose (io-evt fd 'output) (%close-watch-evt w)) and unwatches on nack, exactly as %port-reader's wait does.

**Evidence.** Repro (scratchpad/probe-close3.scm): pre-fill a pipe until EAGAIN so the writer blocks in the event's initial io-evt wait; (spawn (sync (write-string-evt op "hello"))); after 0.2s (close-output-port op) then (create-pipe), whose read end reuses the old fd number. Output: '(old-fd 5 reused-as-read-end? #t)' then 'WRITER-STUCK' -- the 3-second sync/timeout on the writer's result ivar expired; the writer never raised and never woke. Control experiments: the same close on a mid-write wait (probe-close.scm close2) raises 'raised', and a blocked reader (close1) raises at 101ms via the close sweep, so only this initial wait lacks the protection.

**Independent verification.** Code reading: /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.cml.scm line 3009 (write-string-evt fd path) and line 2908 (output-evt fd path) are bare `(wrap (io-evt fd 'output) ...)` waits with no %close-watch!, while %port-output-wait (lines 2928-2933, used for chunks 2..n and the lock-busy case) and %port-reader's wait (lines 2739-2744) both register one; the comments at 2924-2927 and the test test/closed-port-with-a-reused-descriptor (src/test/cml-io.scm ~line 460) document the guarantee that a wait ends when another thread closes the port precisely because the fd number may be reused. That existing test only exercises the between-chunks wait (empty pipe, 200000-char string), never the initial wait on a full pipe. Reproduced with the pre-built scratch repo and /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi. (1) scratchpad/verify-close3.scm: fill pipe to EAGAIN (65536 bytes), spawn (sync (write-string-evt out "hello")) so it blocks in the initial io-evt wait, close-output-port after 0.2s, create-pipe reuses old write fd as new read end. Output: `(filled 65536 bytes r 4 w 5)` / `(old-w 5 new-r 5 new-w 6 reused-as-read-end? #t writer WRITER-STUCK)` — the writer neither raised nor woke within the 3s timeout. (2) scratchpad/verify-close-extra.scm case A, same scenario with (sync (output-evt out)): `(A-output-evt (reused? #t result STUCK))` — line 2908 is equally affected. (3) Control, verify-close-mid.scm: writer blocked between chunks (empty pipe, 200000 chars), same close + fd reuse: `(reused? #t writer port is closed)` — the close watch wakes and raises there, so only the initial waits lack the protection. (4) Control, verify-close-extra.scm case B, initial wait + close WITHOUT fd reuse: `(B-no-reuse (result port is closed))` — confirms the masking claim (file-select reports the closed fd ready, %port-write-chunks! then sees port-closed? and raises), so the bug surfaces only under fd reuse.

#### [MINOR] `aux.cml.scm:622` — %map-event bypasses the guard/with-nack event-type checks

%force* deliberately validates that a guard thunk or a with-nack body returns an event and raises the clear errors "cml: guard did not return an event" (line 677) and "cml: with-nack did not return an event" (line 681). But wrap and wrap-handler rewrite the event tree through %map-event (lines 616-624), whose 'guard case ((guard) (make-%event 'guard (τ (recur (p))))) and 'nack case ((nack) (make-%event 'nack (λ (n) (recur (p n))))) pass the thunk's return value straight to recur, which immediately calls %event-payload on it. So once a guard or with-nack is wrapped, a non-event return no longer reaches the friendly check: the user gets a cryptic record-accessor error instead. The same unchecked %event-tag access makes (choose 42 (always-evt 1)) crash with the identical cryptic message at construction time. Fix: have %map-event's recur (and choose*'s gather) check (event? evt) first and raise the same messages %force* uses, e.g. in the guard case (let1 (e (p)) (unless (event? e) (error "cml: guard did not return an event" e)) (recur e)) and symmetrically for nack.

**Evidence.** Repro with csi (compiled module):
  (sync (guard (lambda () 42)))            => error "cml: guard did not return an event" (42)   [intended]
  (sync (wrap (guard (lambda () 42)) add1)) => error "bad argument type - not a structure of the required type" (42 aux.cml#%event)   [check bypassed]
  (sync (wrap (with-nack (lambda (n) 42)) add1)) => same cryptic error
  (sync (choose 42 (always-evt 1)))         => same cryptic error
Run via probe-wrapped-guard.scm in the scratchpad; all four outputs observed as shown.

**Independent verification.** Read aux.cml.scm lines 605-700: %map-event's recur binds (%event-payload evt) before dispatching on tag and passes guard/nack thunk results straight to recur; choose*'s gather calls %event-tag on raw args; %force* (lines 677-682) has explicit (event? e) checks with friendly messages. Reproduced with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi on the compiled module (script verify-map-event.scm in the scratchpad, each case inside run-cml): "plain guard 42 => ERROR: cml: guard did not return an event (42)"; "wrapped guard 42 => ERROR: bad argument type - not a structure of the required type (42 aux.cml#%event)"; "wrapped with-nack 42 => same"; "choose 42 always => same" (also at bare construction time, without sync). All four outputs match the reviewer's evidence exactly. No test in src/test/cml.scm covers the "did not return an event" messages, and the deliberate checks in %force* show friendly errors are intended, so the bypass is a genuine defect.

#### [MINOR] `aux.cml.scm:1732` — mvar-get-evt/mvar-swap-evt never reset the cell priority: a full mvar permanently starves competing choice branches

Every poll of a SyncVar event bumps the cell's priority counter (%cell-bump! returns the old value and increments), and the commit is supposed to reset it to 1, as ivar-get-evt's do-thunk does (line 1675), as channels, mailboxes and cvars do. The do-thunks of mvar-get-evt (line 1732: (make-%enabled (%cell-bump! mv) (tau (%atomic-end) v))) and mvar-swap-evt (line 1757) never reset it. This is faithful to SML/NJ (sync-var.sml's mGetEvt/mSwapEvt/mTakeEvt doFns all lack 'priority := 1'), but the port's own comment documents the omission only for mvar-take-evt (line 1697), where it is self-limiting because a take empties the mvar. For get/swap the mvar stays full, so the priority grows monotonically without bound and %select-do-fn (max priority wins) then picks the mvar branch on EVERY sync in which it is enabled: any other enabled branch of the choice is starved completely, forever, and the counter itself grows one per poll for the mvar's lifetime. Since the port deliberately fixes several ML fairness/leak bugs elsewhere (waiter leaks, barrier wake order, wrapHandler cross-branch catch), resetting the priority in these two do-thunks (or at least documenting the hazard as done for take) is the consistent fix.

**Evidence.** Repro (scratchpad/probe-fair.scm): 1000 iterations of (sync (choose (wrap (ivar-get-evt a) ...) (wrap (ivar-get-evt b) ...))) over two full ivars split 500/500 (both reset on commit, so ties persist and the tie-break counter alternates). The same loop over a full mvar vs a full ivar -- (choose (mvar-get-evt mv) (ivar-get-evt iv)) -- gives (mvar 999 ivar 1): after the mvar branch's first win its never-reset priority exceeds the ivar's reset-to-1 priority and it wins every remaining sync. SML source check: /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/smlnj/libraries/cml/src/core-cml/sync-var.sml lines 268-288 (mGetEvt) and 315-336 (mSwapEvt) confirm ML also omits the reset, while iGetEvt line 172 has 'priority := 1'.

**Independent verification.** 1) Code reading, /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.cml.scm: ivar-get-evt's do-thunk (line 1675) is (make-%enabled (%cell-bump! iv) (τ (%cell-priority-set! iv 1) (%atomic-end) v)) — it resets priority to 1 on commit, as %cell-put! does (line 1634) and as channels do (%channel-priority-set! ch 1 at 1160/1171/1209/1244). mvar-get-evt (line 1732) and mvar-swap-evt (line 1757) only bump: (make-%enabled (%cell-bump! mv) (τ (%atomic-end) v)) and (make-%enabled (%cell-bump! mv) (τ (%cell-value-set! mv new) (%atomic-end) v)) — no reset. %select-do-fn (line 747) picks the max priority, so an ever-growing priority wins every choice. The port's comment documents the omission only for mvar-take-evt (line 1697: "its doFn does not reset the priority"), where a take empties the mvar so the priority cannot keep climbing; get/swap leave the mvar full.
2) Live repro (scratchpad/verify-mvar-prio.scm, run with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi -q -s against the compiled scratch repo): 1000 syncs of (choose (wrap A ...) (wrap B ...)) over two full ivars give (500 . 500) — fair, both reset. The same loop with a full mvar via mvar-get-evt vs a full ivar gives (999 . 1), and via mvar-swap-evt gives (998 . 2): after the mvar branch's first win its never-reset priority exceeds the ivar's reset-to-1 priority and it wins every remaining sync. Output verbatim: "ivar-vs-ivar (a . b): (500 . 500) / mvarget-vs-ivar (mv . iv): (999 . 1) / mvarswap-vs-ivar (mv . iv): (998 . 2)".
3) SML source check, /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/smlnj/libraries/cml/src/core-cml/sync-var.sml: iGetEvt's doFn (line ~172) has "priority := 1"; mGetEvt's doFn (line ~283) is "fn () => (S.atomicEnd(); v)" and mSwapEvt's (line ~331) is "fn () => (value := SOME newV; S.atomicEnd(); v)" — both lack the reset, so the port is faithful to upstream ML here.

#### [MINOR] `aux.cml.scm:1979` — Barrier misuse errors lost when competing with an enabled branch

barrier-wait-evt reports the two misuse cases -- 'barrier wait after resignation' (line 1979) and 'multiple barrier waits' (lines 1980-1981) -- by returning an ENABLED error event at fixed priority -1 whose do-thunk raises (exn cml barrier). Because the error is just one more enabled branch of the sync, it only raises when it wins the selection. When the sibling branch is also enabled at fixed priority, the misuse is silently swallowed: (a) a select of two barrier-wait-evts on the SAME enrollment, where that wait is the last arrival (so the first poll is enabled), completes the round and returns the new state instead of raising -- this directly contradicts the module's own contract comment at lines 1892-1895 ('two waits on the same enrollment in one sync raise "multiple barrier waits" as a second Barrier.wait does in ML'; ML's second wait raises deterministically); (b) a resigned enrollment's wait in a select with an always-evt returns the always branch's value, never reporting the resignation misuse. The blocked case does raise correctly (the error event is then the only enabled branch). Suggested fix: raise directly from the poll thunk instead of returning an enabled error event -- a poll that raises is already a supported path that abandons the sync and sets the nacks made so far (see the handler in %sync-on-grp at line 853 and the comment at lines 843-844), and it makes the diagnostic deterministic, matching both the contract comment and ML's Barrier.wait. The existing test at test/cml.scm:1347 (waiting-in-another-sync case) still passes with that change since the error is then raised in the same sync that polls.

**Evidence.** Ran with csi (compiled module): (run-cml (lambda () (let* ((b (make-barrier 0 add1)) (e (barrier-enroll b))) (cml/shutdown (condition-case (select (barrier-wait-evt e) (barrier-wait-evt e)) ((exn cml barrier) 'barrier-error)))))) => 1 (round completed, NO error), while the same select with the first wait blocked (a second enrollment outstanding) => barrier-error. Also (barrier-resign e) then (select (barrier-wait-evt e) (always-evt 'ok)) => ok on every run (5/5), never the documented 'barrier wait after resignation' error. Mechanism: in %select-do-fn (line 747) every fixed priority -1 maps to n (the count of enabled events), so the error branch merely ties with any other fixed-priority enabled branch and the deterministic tie-break counter picks the other one.

**Independent verification.** Read aux.cml.scm lines 1975-1994 (barrier-wait-evt returns make-%enabled -1 with the error thunk for both misuse cases), lines 747-757 (%select-do-fn maps fixed prio -1 to n, ties broken by the deterministic %random counter), lines 1892-1894 (contract comment promising 'multiple barrier waits' raises as in ML), and lines 843-859 (%sync-on-grp's poll handler showing a raising poll is a supported path, as the suggested fix relies on). Reproduced with the compiled module (scratchpad/barrier-repro.scm, run 3x, identical output): case-a [(select (barrier-wait-evt e) (barrier-wait-evt e)) on one enrollment, last arrival] => 1, round completed with NO error, directly contradicting the contract comment; same select with the wait blocked => barrier-error. case-b [(barrier-resign e) then (select (barrier-wait-evt e) (always-evt 'ok))] => ok, misuse swallowed; reversing the branch order => barrier-error, proving the outcome is a tie-break accident of branch order. Plain sync of a resigned wait => barrier-error (error is the only branch), as claimed.

#### [MINOR] `aux.cml.scm:2110` — Logged servers' shutdown thunks get 1 second, not the documented 2

cml/log-server! documents that at shutdown each server's shut thunk runs "at most 2 seconds" (comment line 2094, and %clean-servers line 2110 waits with (timeout-evt 2) per server). But %clean-servers itself runs as the "Servers" cleaner through %cleanup-run (line 2073), which gives EVERY cleaner only (timeout-evt 1). After 1 second the root context abandons the whole Servers cleaner — all logged servers together — runs the remaining cleaners, and run-cml then calls %reset! and returns, freezing the scheduler, so the still-running shut thunks are cut off mid-work. The inner 2-second budget is unreachable; a shut function that flushes or closes resources sized against the documented 2 s is silently truncated at 1 s (and n slow servers share one second in total, not 2 s each). AUX-CML.md section 4.11 repeats the "at most 2 seconds" promise. Fix options: run the shutdown-side Servers phase outside the generic 1-second cap (e.g. give %cleanup-run a per-cleaner timeout, 1 + 2*n or simply larger for "Servers"), or drop the inner select and document 1 second.

**Evidence.** Repro (probe-server-timeout.scm): (cml/log-server! "slow" void (lambda () (cml/sleep 1.5) (set! done #t))), then (run-cml (lambda () (cml/shutdown 'ok))). Output: "shutdown took 1002 ms; slow server shut completed? #f" — the shut thunk was abandoned at 1 s and never finished, though it slept only 1.5 s, within the documented 2-second allowance.

**Independent verification.** Code: src/aux.cml.scm line 2073 wraps EVERY cleaner in (select (join-evt (spawn/call ...)) (timeout-evt 1)); line 2201 registers %clean-servers as the "Servers" cleaner, so its per-server (timeout-evt 2) at line 2110 sits inside a 1-second envelope and is unreachable as a wait budget; after %cleanup-run 'at-shutdown returns, run-cml (lines 2163-2169) calls %reset! and returns, freezing the abandoned shut threads. Docs promise 2 s in four places: comment line 2094, AUX-CML.md lines 545-550, 1248 (section 4.11), and 1885 (known-limitations, which even implies shutdowns up to 2 s ARE waited for). Repro run with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi against the scratch repo (script: scratchpad/probe-server-timeout.scm): a shut thunk doing (cml/sleep 1.5) then (set! done #t) — within the documented 2 s — printed "shutdown took 1002 ms; slow server shut completed? #f"; a 0.5 s control thunk printed "fast shutdown took 502 ms; fast server shut completed? #t". The 1.5 s thunk was abandoned at ~1 s and never finished, exactly as claimed.

### aux.category.ziplist.scm

#### [MAJOR] `aux.category.ziplist.scm:16` — ziplist >>= silently drops #f elements via length/>? cond test

In the else branch of >>=, presence of the i-th element of the inner list m* is tested with (cond ((length/>? m* i) => (λ (v*) ...)) (else skip)). length/>? returns the ELEMENT at index i when the list is long enough, and #f when it is too short — so a stored #f element is indistinguishable from absence and the pair for position i is silently dropped from the diagonal. Any ziplist computation whose body produces #f (predicates being the obvious case) returns a too-short, misaligned result. Fix: use a helper that returns the tail pair at index i (a pair is never #f), e.g. (let ((t (list-tail/or-false m* i))) (if t (cons i* (cons (car t) acc)) (cons i* acc))); the helper must terminate on the circular lists produced by return, exactly as length/>? does (walk decrementing i, stop at null or i=0).

**Evidence.** (import (aux base) (aux category ziplist) (aux category monad ziplist)) (do/monad (← x '(1 2 3)) ,(even? x)) => (#t) instead of (#f #t #f); raw (>>= '(1 2) (λ (v) (list #f #f))) => () instead of (#f #f). Non-#f diagonals are fine: (do/monad (← x '(1 2 3)) (← y '(a b)) ,(list x y)) => ((1 a) (2 b)) and (<*> (list add1 sub1 sub1) (list 1 2 3)) => (2 1 2).

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.category.ziplist.scm:16 and aux.base.scm:402-406: length/>? indeed returns the ELEMENT at index n ((0 . (,v . _)) v) or #f when short, and the ziplist >>= else-branch discriminates on its truthiness via (cond (... => ...)), so a stored #f element hits the skip branch. Ran the reviewer's exact repro against the pre-compiled scratch repo with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi (script at .../scratchpad/ziplist-repro.scm). Output: "predicate diagonal: (#t)" for (do/monad (← x '(1 2 3)) ,(even? x)) — expected (#f #t #f); "raw >>= with #f: ()" for (>>= '(1 2) (λ (v) (list #f #f))) — expected (#f #f); non-#f cases correct: ((1 a) (2 b)) and (2 1 2). All four reviewer data points reproduced verbatim. Additionally, the repo's own audit AUDIT-base-kanren.md (section around line 64-86) independently documents this same defect ("the #f at index 0 vanished ... the ziplist monad loses #f values", verdict "reproduced"), proposes a boolean-returning length/>? fix, and notes no test covers ziplist — so this is a known bug, not intended semantics.

#### [MAJOR] `aux.category.ziplist.scm:17` — ziplist >>= compacts skipped indices, breaking alignment and associativity

In the else branch of >>= (lines 12-18), when (g v_i) has fewer than i+1 elements the cond's else keeps the incremented index but conses nothing, so later indices that DO have an element slide left into the gap. The result is a compacted list rather than a diagonal truncated at the first missing element, so positions no longer correspond to input indices and subsequent zips pair wrong elements. This makes the monad associativity law fail outright (255 violations in 2000 random trials): lhs and rhs even have different lengths. Note this is distinct from the already-reported #f-conflation in length/>?: no #f elements are involved here — the counterexamples use short-but-valid inner lists. Fix: on a missing i-th element, stop the fold (truncate) instead of skipping, which restores index alignment (and makes the fuzz test pass in my re-run with a truncating variant).

**Evidence.** (z:>>= '(1 2 3) g) with g = (λ (v) (if (= v 2) '(a) '(x y z w))) returns (x z): index 1 has no element yet index 2's z is emitted at position 1. Associativity fuzz (2000 trials, random ragged tables): 255 violations, e.g. m=(7 4 3 6), f/g via lookup tables => lhs=(5 6 0 5) vs rhs=(5 6 0).

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.category.ziplist.scm (lines 12-18) and length/>? in src/aux.base.scm:402-406: the else branch `(else (cons i* acc))` does increment the index while consing nothing, so a later present diagonal element slides left into the gap — the claim matches the code as written. Ran the exact repro with the compiled scratch repo (/home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi, scratchpad/zip-repro.scm): `(>>= '(1 2 3) g)` with g = (λ (v) (if (= v 2) '(a) '(x y z w))) prints `(x z)` — index 2's z emitted at position 1, exactly as claimed. Associativity fuzz (2000 trials, ragged lookup-table f/g, lists ≤5 over values 0-7): 574/2000 violations with the shipped >>=, including length mismatches (e.g. m=(6 2 4 0 7): lhs=(4 2) vs rhs=(4)), matching the reviewer's 255/2000 in spirit. No tests exist for ziplist (grep of src/test/ finds nothing); the module's own block-comment examples (do/monad over ragged lists => ((1 a c d) (2 b c* e)), <*> => (2 1 2)) still pass with current code because their gaps occur only at the tail, so they show zip/positional intent without discriminating skip vs truncate — but that positional intent is what compaction breaks.

#### [MINOR] `aux.category.ziplist.scm:8` — Pure ziplist do/monad result is circular; printing it never terminates

return builds a genuinely circular list via list/circular, and >>='s first clause propagates g's result unchanged, so any do/monad chain whose subject is a return (e.g. every clause uses the ,expr sugar) evaluates to a circular list at top level. CHICKEN's write/display has no cycle detection, so printing the result — which the module's own usage example does at the REPL ('(define l (do/monad (← x ,3) ...)) l') — spews output forever and freezes the session. The circular representation is needed for the monad laws, but the hazard deserves mitigation: either wrap ziplists in a record with a cycle-aware printer (as difflist and writer already do), or document that pure results must be forced through a bind with a finite list before printing.

**Evidence.** (write (do/monad (← x ,3) (← y ,4) ,(+ x y))) with the ziplist monad printed '(7 7 7 ...' and produced over 64MB of output in under 5 seconds before being killed by timeout; it never terminates.

**Independent verification.** Ran /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi with the scratch repository. Script repro: (define l (do/monad (← x ,3) (← y ,4) ,(+ x y))) gives pair? #t, car 7, (eq? (cdr l) l) #t; then (write l) printed '(7 7 7 ...' and produced 151,580,672 bytes before the 10s timeout killed it (exit 124) — write has no cycle detection and never terminates. However, piping the module's own comment-block usage example into interactive csi does NOT hang: the REPL printer is cycle-aware and prints '#0=(7 . #0#)', exiting 0 with 2 KB of output. Code reading confirms return = (list/circular x) (src/aux.category.ziplist.scm:8) and >>='s first clause ((,l . ,,m) (g l)) propagating g's result; AUDIT-base-kanren.md documents the ,,m circular-list detection as intentional.

#### [MINOR] `aux.category.ziplist.scm:12` — ziplist >>= loops forever on multi-element circular lists

The pure-recognition clause ((,l . ,,m) (g l)) at line 11 only matches a ONE-element circular list (cdr eqv? to the whole list), which is what return builds via list/circular. Any circular list with 2+ elements — constructible with the very same exported (aux base) list/circular that return uses, and a natural way to write an infinite-repeat ziplist — falls into the else branch, whose foldl walks the circular spine and never terminates while allocating unboundedly. A cycle-safe guard (or restricting the else branch with a proper-list check and raising otherwise) would turn the hang into an error or correct diagonal behavior.

**Evidence.** Ran with csi under timeout 10: (>>= (list/circular 1 2) (lambda (x) (list x x))) never returns; process killed by timeout (exit 124) while (>>= (return 10) ...) and all finite cases work.

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.category.ziplist.scm (lines 8-18) and aux.base.scm:402-414 (length/>?, list/circular). The clause ((,l . ,,m) (g l)) matches only a pair whose cdr is eqv? to m itself, i.e. the 1-element circular list return builds; any longer circular list hits the else branch whose foldl traverses the circular spine without termination. Reproduced with the compiled scratch repo: script importing (aux base) and (aux category ziplist) printed return-case (10 #t), (>>= (return 10) (lambda (x) (list x (* x x)))) => (10 100), (>>= '(1 2 3) ...) => (1 2), then (>>= (list/circular 1 2) (lambda (x) (list x x))) hung until timeout 10 killed csi with exit 124. No ziplist test suite exists (nothing under src/test matches ziplist), and no comment restricts inputs to proper lists or 1-element cycles, so the hang is neither tested nor documented as intended.

### aux.simdjson.scm

#### [MAJOR] `aux.simdjson.scm:84` — simdjson-load leaks the entire malloc'd DOM tree on every call

(define (simdjson-load filename) (simdjson->scheme (simdjson-load-ondemand-callback filename))) never calls simdjson-free on the returned tree, so every call leaks the whole malloc'd intermediate DOM (every node, value union, key and string buffer). simdjson-load/ondemand (line 86) does free it and is otherwise identical. Fix: make simdjson-load an alias for simdjson-load/ondemand. Related smaller leak on the error path: simdjson-load/ondemand and simdjson-parse/ondemand skip simdjson-free when simdjson->scheme raises (reachable today: a big integer literal like 123456789012345678901234567890 yields CHICKEN_SIMDJSON_TYPE_UNKNOWN and (error "Unknown simdjson type")); wrapping the convert+free in dynamic-wind fixes that too.

**Evidence.** RSS measured in one csi process on a 60-byte document: after 1000 simdjson-load 11444 kB; after 21000 simdjson-load 34396 kB (~1.1 kB leaked per call, linear); the following 40000 simdjson-load/ondemand calls only moved RSS 34396->35400 kB. Big-int repro for the error path: (simdjson-parse/ondemand "[123456789012345678901234567890]") => Error: Unknown simdjson type 0 — and the dom pointer is never freed.

**Independent verification.** Read src/aux.simdjson.scm (line 84: simdjson-load converts the tree returned by simdjson-load-ondemand-callback and never calls simdjson-free, while simdjson-load/ondemand at 86-90 does) and src/chicken-simdjson.cpp (chicken_simdjson_visit_ondemand mallocs every node, value union, key and string; only chicken_simdjson_free releases them). Live repro with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi against the scratch repo, 45-byte doc: baseline VmRSS 10156 kB; after 1000 simdjson-load 11276 kB; after 21000 simdjson-load 30784 kB (~975 B/call, linear); the following 40000 simdjson-load/ondemand calls moved RSS only to 31160 kB (allocator noise). Error path also reproduced: (simdjson-parse/ondemand "[123456789012345678901234567890]") => "Error: Unknown simdjson type 0 #<pointer 0x62badad0c250>", raised between simdjson->scheme and simdjson-free at lines 94-95, so that dom leaks too. Tests (src/test/test.scm:169) assert simdjson-load and simdjson-load/ondemand return equal values, so aliasing them is semantics-preserving; nothing documents the leak as intended.

#### [MAJOR] `aux.simdjson.scm:93` — simdjson-parse/ondemand passes character count as byte length, truncating UTF-8 input

(simdjson-parse-ondemand-callback str (string-length str)): in CHICKEN 6 strings are UTF-8 and string-length returns the CHARACTER count, but the C side (padded_string(data, length)) needs the BYTE length. Any JSON text containing a non-ASCII character is truncated by one byte per multi-byte character, so the closing brackets are cut off and the parse fails (today that failure is the process-killing abort from the exception bug; with that fixed it would still be a spurious parse error, and in unlucky cases silently wrong data when the truncated text is still valid JSON). Fix: pass the byte length — e.g. drop the length parameter and use strlen(data) in C (valid JSON text can never contain a raw NUL byte, so strlen is exact), or compute (bytevector-length (string->utf8 str)) in Scheme.

**Evidence.** (simdjson-parse/ondemand "{\"k\": \"cafè\"}") — 14 bytes, string-length 13 — dies with INCOMPLETE_ARRAY_OR_OBJECT: 'JSON document ended early in the middle of an object or array' (then aborts via the exception bug). The identical document with 'cafe' parses fine.

**Independent verification.** Code read: /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.simdjson.scm line 93 is exactly `(simdjson-parse-ondemand-callback str (string-length str))`, and the C side (/data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/chicken-simdjson.cpp:195-199) does `simdjson::padded_string my_padded_data(data, length)` — a byte count. Repro with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi against the pre-compiled scratch repo: (1) `(string-length "{\"k\": \"cafè\"}")` => 13 while `(bytevector-length (string->utf8 ...))` => 14, so the C side sees one byte too few; (2) `(simdjson-parse/ondemand "{\"k\": \"cafe\"}")` => `((k cafe))` (works); (3) `(simdjson-parse/ondemand "{\"k\": \"cafè\"}")` => `terminate called after throwing an instance of 'simdjson::simdjson_error' / what(): INCOMPLETE_ARRAY_OR_OBJECT: JSON document ended early in the middle of an object or array` — process Aborted (core dumped), exit 134. This matches the reviewer's claimed evidence character for character. No test in src/test/ exercises non-ASCII JSON, and nothing documents ASCII-only input as intended.

#### [MINOR] `aux.simdjson.scm:47` — JSON strings containing \u0000 are silently truncated at the NUL

simdjson-get-string is declared with return type c-string, so the value is copied with strlen semantics and everything after an embedded NUL byte is silently dropped. JSON strings may legally contain \u0000, and the C side already knows the true length (str->size() in chicken-simdjson.cpp line 96) but throws it away by NUL-terminating. Fix: store the length in the node (e.g. reuse the many_t length field or add one) and return a sized bytevector/string via a wrapper that calls C_utf_string with the stored length.

**Evidence.** (simdjson-parse/ondemand "{\"k\": \"a\\u0000b\"}") => ((k "a")), string-length 1: the 'b' after the NUL is silently lost.

**Independent verification.** Reproduced with the compiled module: (import (aux simdjson)) (simdjson-parse/ondemand "{\"k\": \"a\\u0000b\"}") printed ((k "a")) with string-length 1 — the 'b' after the embedded NUL is silently dropped. Code reading confirms the mechanism: /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/chicken-simdjson.cpp lines 98-105 copy the full string with str->copy(buf, length) (length = str->size(), so the NUL and following bytes ARE in the C buffer) and then NUL-terminate, discarding the length; /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.simdjson.scm line 47 declares simdjson-get-string with return type c-string, so CHICKEN marshals it with strlen semantics and truncates at the first NUL. Also verified CHICKEN 6 strings can hold NULs ((string-length "a\x00;b") => 3), so the loss is purely at this FFI boundary and the proposed fix (return a sized string using the stored length) is feasible. No test in src/test/ mentions NUL or documents truncation as intended.

#### [MINOR] `aux.simdjson.scm:98` — ->string/json emits invalid JSON for control chars, exact ratios, non-finite floats and quoted keys

Four ways ->string/json produces output no JSON parser accepts: (1) strings are emitted with Scheme write (line 104), whose escapes only coincide with JSON for the common cases — a control character becomes CHICKEN's \x01; escape, which is not JSON; (2) object keys are emitted with display and no escaping at all (line 124), so a key containing a double quote produces {"ke"y":1}; (3) exact rationals print as 1/3 (line 102); (4) +inf.0/-inf.0/+nan.0 print literally. Fix: one shared JSON string writer (escape ", \, and codepoints <0x20 as \uXXXX) used for both keys and string values, exact->inexact (or error) for non-fixnum exact numbers, and an error (or null) for non-finite floats.

**Evidence.** csi: (->string/json (vector (string (integer->char 1)) "tab\there")) => ["\x01;","tab\there"]; (->string/json 1/3) => 1/3; (->string/json +inf.0) => +inf.0; (->string/json (list (list "ke\"y" 1))) => {"ke"y":1}. All four rejected by any JSON parser.

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.simdjson.scm lines 98-130: strings go through Scheme `write` (line 104), numbers through `display` (line 102), and object keys through raw `display` with no escaping (line 124) — exactly as claimed. Ran all four repros with the custom csi against the pre-compiled scratch repo:
  (->string/json (vector (string (integer->char 1)) "tab\there")) => ["\x01;","tab\there"]   (\x01; is not a JSON escape)
  (->string/json 1/3)   => 1/3
  (->string/json +inf.0) => +inf.0   (-inf.0 and +nan.0 likewise print literally)
  (->string/json (list (list "ke\"y" 1))) => {"ke"y":1}
All four are tokens/strings no JSON parser accepts. Note the "tab\there" case itself is fine (\t is valid JSON); only the control-char escape form is wrong, matching the claim's wording.

### aux.base.scm

#### [MAJOR] `aux.base.scm:402` — length/>? conflates a #f element with a too-short list

length/>? returns the element AFTER the n-prefix as its truthy witness, so a list longer than n whose (n+1)-th element is #f is reported as NOT longer — a ?-named exported predicate that answers wrongly for any list carrying #f at that position. Two in-repo consequences: (1) pairwise-different? (aux.base.scm:285) then silently falls off the hash path onto the O(n²) scan — still correct, but a measured 121x slowdown on 30k elements triggered by nothing more than a #f at index 60; (2) aux.category.ziplist.scm:16 uses (cond ((length/>? m* i) => ...)) to fetch the i-th element of a monadic value, so a legitimate #f element there is treated as list-exhausted and dropped from the result — a correctness bug rooted here. Fix: make length/>? return the remaining tail pair instead of the element ((0 . (,v . ,r)) → the pair itself, which is always truthy); pairwise-different? needs no change and ziplist takes (car v*).

**Evidence.** csi: (length/>? (list 1 2 #f 4) 2) → #f although the list has length 4 > 2. Benchmark, 30000 distinct elements: element 60 truthy → hash path 0.021s CPU; same list with #f at index 60 → scan path 2.543s CPU (121x).

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.base.scm:402-406 — length/>? indeed returns the (n+1)-th element `v` as its witness, so a #f there is indistinguishable from a too-short list. Ran repro with the pre-compiled modules (/home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi + scratch CHICKEN_REPOSITORY_PATH): (length/>? (list 1 2 #f 4) 2) => #f although length 4 > 2, while (length/>? (list 1 2 3 4) 2) => 3. Ziplist consequence reproduced: (>>= '(a b c) (lambda (x) (if (eq? x 'b) (list 1 #f 3) (list x x x)))) => (a c) — the legitimate #f element at position 1 is silently dropped; correct ziplist semantics give (a #f c) (src/aux.category.ziplist.scm:16 uses (cond ((length/>? m* i) => ...))). Benchmark of pairwise-different? on 30000 distinct elements: hash path 0.022s CPU; same data with #f at index 60 forces the O(n²) scan path, 2.562s CPU (~116x, matching the reviewer's ~121x). No tests cover length/>? or ziplist (grep of src/test/ empty).

#### [MINOR] `aux.base.scm:90` — Non-final else in match/first silently becomes the literal pattern else

match-case-simple* only recognizes an (else expr ...) clause when it is the LAST clause (line 90's rule has no trailing clauses). An else clause written anywhere earlier falls through to the generic (pattern expr ...) rule (line 98), where the identifier else is compiled as a literal symbol pattern: it matches only when the value is the symbol else, and the intended catch-all is silently skipped while later clauses keep running. No error is raised at expansion or at run time, so a misplaced else quietly changes program behavior. Fix: add a rule before the generic one — ((match-case-simple* val (else expr ...) clause0 clause ...) (syntax-error "match/first: else clause must be last")) — turning the mistake into an expansion-time error. The same guard belongs in dmatch-aux for match/non-overlapping, which has no else handling at all.

**Evidence.** csi repro: (match/first 5 (else 'fallback) (,x (list 'var x))) => (var 5) — the else clause is bypassed; (match/first 'else (else 'fallback) (,x (list 'var x))) => fallback — it fires only for the symbol else, proving it was compiled as a literal pattern.

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.base.scm lines 85-100: rule at line 90 `((match-case-simple* val (else expr ...)) (begin expr ...))` indeed accepts else only as the LAST clause; a non-final else falls to the generic rule at line 98 where `pattern` is a plain syntax-rules pattern variable (the `else` literal in the literals list is irrelevant there since it does not appear literally in that rule's pattern), and match-pattern's final rule (line 126) compiles it as `(equal? val 'else)`. Ran the repro in csi 6.0.1pre1 (scratchpad/else-repro.scm, imported (aux base) from the scratch repo): (match/first 5 (else 'fallback) (,x (list 'var x))) => "(var 5)" (else silently bypassed); (match/first 'else (else 'fallback) (,x ...)) => "fallback" (fires only for the literal symbol else); final else works normally => "fallback". Also verified dmatch-aux (lines 50-64): its literals are only (⊣ =>), so match/non-overlapping has no else handling at all, exactly as claimed. Tests (src/test/dmatch.scm lines 104-105) use else only in final position, so no in-repo code is affected.

#### [MINOR] `aux.base.scm:156` — (μ () body) expands to a unary function, not a thunk

The first μ clause is ((μ () body ...) (λ (v) body ...)): an empty formal list produces a one-argument procedure whose parameter v is a hygienic name the body cannot even reference. So ((μ () 42)) raises an arity error while ((μ () 42) 'anything) returns 42 — the opposite of what the curried-lambda reading of μ suggests (zero variables → zero-argument function, i.e. τ). If 'takes one ignored argument' were the intent, λ_ already provides it. Fix: make the () clause expand to (τ body ...) — or drop the clause so (μ ()) is a syntax error — keeping (μ (v) ...) and the curried clauses as they are.

**Evidence.** csi with the compiled (aux base): ((μ () 42)) => Error: bad argument count - received 0 but expected 1; ((μ () 42) 'x) => 42. No in-repo caller currently writes (μ ()) (grep hits only the definition), so the fix is compatible.

**Independent verification.** Read aux.base.scm:154-159: clause ((μ () body ...) (λ (v) body ...)) with hygienic, unreferencable v — matches the claim verbatim. Repro with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi and the compiled scratch repo: ((μ () 42) 'x) => 42; ((μ () 42)) raises, message captured via (get-condition-property e 'exn 'message) => "bad argument count - received 0 but expected 1". Grep of all sources, tests, README and AUX-*.md finds no caller of (μ ()) (only the definition and aux.base.import.scm) and no documentation of μ, so no intended-semantics defense exists; the curried clauses and the adjacent τ/λ_ macros support the reviewer's reading.

### aux.category.difflist.scm

#### [MAJOR] `aux.category.difflist.scm:24` — difflist fail is a wall, not the empty difflist: it discards its tail

(define (fail . args) (make-category-difflist (λ (xs) '()))) — a difference list is a function that PREPENDS its content to the tail it receives; the empty difflist must be the identity (λ (xs) xs) (which is exactly mempty at line 27). Returning '() unconditionally throws away the received tail, so (mappend (fail) d) erases d entirely, and inside >>= (whose foldr composes the per-element difflists) a fail-produced element erases everything composed to its right plus the eventual tail. This is inconsistent with the list-monad fail ('() = mempty), where a failing branch drops only itself. Fix: (define (fail . args) mempty) — observable behavior of difflist->list on a lone fail is unchanged ('()), but composition becomes correct.

**Evidence.** (import (prefix (aux category difflist) D:)) (D:difflist->list (D:mappend (D:fail) (D:list->difflist '(1 2)))) => () but should be (1 2). The mirror case (D:mappend (D:list->difflist '(1 2)) (D:fail)) => (1 2) only by accident of application order.

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.category.difflist.scm: line 24 is exactly (define (fail . args) (make-category-difflist (λ (xs) '()))), which ignores the received tail, while mempty at line 27 is (λ (lst) lst). Ran a repro script with the custom csi against the pre-compiled scratch repo; output: [mappend (fail) '(1 2): ()] vs [mappend '(1 2) (fail): (1 2)] — asymmetric erasure exactly as claimed; [>>= difflist '(1 2 3) with fail on 2: (1)] vs list monad [(1 3)] — a mid-stream fail erases everything composed to its right, unlike the list monad where fail is '() (aux.category.list.scm line 10) and drops only itself; [mappend (>>= ... fail-on-2) '(9 9): (1)] — the eventual tail is swallowed too; substituting D:mempty for fail gives (1 3), confirming the proposed fix restores list-monad-consistent behavior. No test in src/test/ references difflist and no comment documents wall semantics, so this is not intended behavior.

#### [MAJOR] `aux.category.difflist.scm:36` — reverse/difflist reverses the received tail along with its own content

(reverse/difflist dl) builds (λ (xs) (reverse ((category-difflist-f dl) xs))): it appends dl's content to the incoming tail xs and then reverses the WHOLE thing, so any difflist later appended after the reversed one gets reversed too and moved to the front. It is only correct when the reversed difflist is the last/only one materialized. Fix: reverse only the own content and prepend it: (λ (xs) (append (reverse ((category-difflist-f dl) '())) xs)) — or fold the content onto xs with cons for a single pass: (foldl (λ (acc x) (cons x acc))-style over (f '()).

**Evidence.** (D:difflist->list (D:mappend (D:reverse/difflist (D:list->difflist '(1 2 3))) (D:list->difflist '(4 5)))) => (5 4 3 2 1) but should be (3 2 1 4 5). Standalone (D:difflist->list (D:reverse/difflist (D:list->difflist '(1 2 3)))) is correct, which is why it looks fine in isolation.

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.category.difflist.scm: line 36 is (make-category-difflist (λ (xs) (reverse ((category-difflist-f dl) xs)))) — it appends dl's content onto the incoming tail xs and reverses the whole result, exactly as claimed. Reproduced with the pre-compiled scratch repo and /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi (script at .../scratchpad/revdl.scm):
  standalone: (3 2 1)                       [correct]
  mappend after reversed: (5 4 3 2 1)       [wrong; should be (3 2 1 4 5)]
  mappend before reversed: (4 5 3 2 1)      [correct, since the reversed one gets '() as tail there]
Also ran the reviewer's proposed fix (λ (xs) (append (reverse ((category-difflist-f dl) '())) xs)) against the same cases: it yields (3 2 1 4 5), (4 5 3 2 1), (3 2 1) — all correct, so the fix is sound.
Intended-semantics check: grep shows no test in src/test/ touches difflist at all; the only in-repo use of reverse/difflist is a commented-out standalone example in src/aux.category.writer.scm line 49 (the one case that happens to work). Nothing documents tail-reversal as intended, and it breaks the difflist invariant (each difflist's function must be λ xs → own-content ++ xs), which mappend (function composition, line 28) relies on.

#### [MINOR] `aux.category.difflist.scm:30` — difflist mlog wraps values in (got ...) unlike the list monoid's mlog

(define (mlog x) (make-category-difflist (λ (xs) (cons `(got ,x) xs)))) tags every logged value with a (got ...) wrapper, while the list monoid's mlog (aux.category.list.scm:16) logs the bare value. mlog is the writer functor's injection primitive (tell = (make-category-writer (M:mlog a) ...)), so the same writer program produces differently-shaped logs depending only on which monoid instance is plugged in — switching writer-list to writer-difflist (the whole point of difflist: O(1) append) silently changes every log entry. The (got ,x) looks like leftover debugging; fix: (λ (xs) (cons x xs)).

**Evidence.** writer over list: (WL:>>= (WL:tell 1) (λ (_) (WL:tell 2))) has log (1 2); writer over difflist: same program's log materializes to ((got 1) (got 2)).

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.category.difflist.scm:30 — (define (mlog x) (make-category-difflist (λ (xs) (cons `(got ,x) xs)))) — vs aux.category.list.scm:16 — (define (mlog x) (list x)). aux.category.writer.scm:31 confirms tell = (make-category-writer (M:mlog a) (void)), so mlog is the writer's injection primitive. Ran repro under the scratch repository with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi (script at scratchpad/difflist-mlog.scm); output:
  list mlog of 'a:     (a)
  difflist mlog of 'a: ((got a))
  writer/list log:     (1 2)
  writer/difflist log: ((got 1) (got 2))
Same program (>>= (tell 1) (λ (_) (tell 2))) produces differently shaped logs depending only on the monoid instance. grep shows no test in src/test/ references difflist and no comment documents the (got ...) shape, so it is not pinned intended behavior; within the module, return (line 18) conses the bare value, making mlog's tagging internally inconsistent too — consistent with leftover debugging.

### aux.machine.secd.scm

#### [MAJOR] `aux.machine.secd.scm:371` — Test instruction drops the rest of the control: non-tail If miscompiled

compile and compile⁺ lay out an If₊ as `question... (Test otherwise-code) answer... rest...` (lines 325 and 400), and the Test rule of →/compiled (lines 371-374) and →/compiled⁺ (lines 471-474) handles the false case with `(make-status S₊ E (%Test-control C₀) D)`: the control is REPLACED by the otherwise-code, so `answer...` AND everything after the If (`rest...`) are silently discarded. The true case is fine (answer runs, then rest), so the machine only works when the If is in tail position of a closure body, where the dropped rest is empty and the dump unwinds correctly (which is why the Y/length tests pass). Any If whose value feeds a surrounding computation in the same control list gives a silently wrong answer, disagreeing with the direct evaluator `value`. In →/compiled⁺ it is worse: a false If inside an Enter/Exit redex body also drops the Exit, so the Enter dump is unwound by the generic pop rule and the surrounding computation is abandoned mid-way. Fix: make Test carry both branches, `(Test answer-code otherwise-code)`, and have the rule prepend the chosen branch to the remaining control: `(make-status S₊ E (append branch C₊) D)` (the classic SECD uses SEL/JOIN with the dump for the same purpose). Note this changes the printed instruction sequences that test/machine.secd.scm test/compile and test/compiled-runs pin down, so those expected strings must be regenerated; the bug is inherited from the original on-scheme code but, unlike the documented J divergence, it is not documented anywhere.

**Evidence.** Ran with the scratch csi (scratchpad/secd-if.scm), env `add1/one=1/two=2/three=3/t=#t/f=#f`: term (add1 (cond (f one) (else two))): value=3, →/compiled=2, →/compiled⁺=2 (add1 dropped); true branch (add1 (cond (t one) (else two))): all three agree on 2. Enter/Exit interaction: (add1 ((λ (x) (cond (x two) (else three))) f)): value=4, →/compiled⁺=3 (Exit and the pending add1/Apply dropped).

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.machine.secd.scm: compile (line 325) and compile⁺ (line 400) lay out If₊ as `question... (Test otherwise-code) answer... rest...`, and both Test rules (lines 371-374 and 471-474) handle false with `(make-status S₊ E (%Test-control C₀) D)`, replacing the entire remaining control — `answer...` and `rest...` are discarded, exactly as claimed. Ran a repro (scratchpad/secd-if-verify.scm) with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi against the scratch repo, env add1/one=1/two=2/three=3/t=#t/f=#f:

term: (add1 (cond (f one) (else two)))  → value: 3, →/compiled: 2, →/compiled⁺: 2   [add1 silently dropped]
term: (add1 (cond (t one) (else two)))  → value: 2, →/compiled: 2, →/compiled⁺: 2   [true branch fine]
term: (add1 ((λ (x) (cond (x two) (else three))) f)) → value: 4, →/compiled: 4, →/compiled⁺: 3   [compile⁺'s Enter/Exit inlining makes the If non-tail; Exit and the pending add1/Apply are dropped, exactly the claimed worse behavior; plain →/compiled is correct here because the If stays in the closure-body tail and the dump unwinds]
term: ((λ (x) (cond (x one) (else two))) f) / ...t) → all three agree (2 and 1)   [tail position works, both branches]

All final statuses were (S=(wrong-value) C=() D=#<unspecified>), i.e. clean termination with a silently wrong answer, no error raised.

#### [MAJOR] `aux.machine.secd.scm:428` — J program-closure with void dump keeps current dump: jump resumes abandoned context

In →/compiled⁺'s PA helper, applying a program-closure whose captured dump D₀ is (void) builds (make-status `(,body ,y . ,S) E (list (Apply)) D) — it keeps the CURRENT dump D (and current stack S). The sibling status? branch correctly REPLACES the machine state with the captured dump's S₁/E₁/C₁/D₁, which is the J semantics (Danvy & Millikin: applying a program closure abandons the current context and reinstates the captured dump). A J captured at top level (D = void at Jump time, i.e. before any Enter) therefore fails to abandon pending contexts whenever the jump is performed inside a non-direct call: after body·y is computed, the empty-C dump-popping rule resumes the caller's pending control instead of halting with body·y. The existing tests don't catch it because in all of them the dumps live at jump-application time are Enter dumps with empty C, so popping through them is harmless. Fix: make the void branch symmetric with the status? branch — discard the current context, e.g. (make-status (list body y) E (list (Apply)) (void)); all existing test answers (2, 4, 5, 5) are preserved by this change. Note this is distinct from both the already-reported Test-drops-control bug and the documented 7→5 divergence (which is about WHICH dump Jump captures, not about how a void-dump program closure is applied); it is plausibly inherited from on-scheme, but it contradicts the machine's own status?-branch semantics.

**Evidence.** Repro (csi, scratchpad/j-jump.scm): term ((λ (k) ((λ (g) (+ (g two) one)) (λ (x) (k x)))) (J (λ (z) z))). k is a program closure capturing the void top-level dump, so (k two) must return ((λ (z) z) two) = 2 as the final program answer, discarding the pending (+ _ one). The machine answers 3: after the jump computes 2, C₁=((Apply)) empties and dump popping resumes the pending [Load +, Apply, Apply] control of the g-application dump, computing (+ 2 1). Output: 'jump-through-lambda: 3 (J semantics expects 2)'; controls: the no-jump variant gives 5 as expected, and the direct-redex variant ((λ (L) (² (L two))) (J (λ (z) z))) gives 2 only because Enter dumps have empty C.

**Independent verification.** Read aux.machine.secd.scm:415-443: PA's program-closure void? branch builds (make-status `(,body ,y . ,S) E (list (Apply)) D), keeping current S and D, while the status? branch reinstates the captured dump's S/E/C/D — the asymmetry the claim describes is present verbatim. Reproduced with the specified csi + scratch repo: the reviewer's term ((λ (k) ((λ (g) (+ (g two) one)) (λ (x) (k x)))) (J (λ (z) z))) answers 3, not the J-semantics 2 — output: "jump-through-lambda: 3 (J semantics expects 2)"; controls behaved as claimed (no-jump 3, direct-redex 2, existing J answers 4/5/5). Then compiled a patched copy in the scratchpad (void branch made symmetric: S=(body y), C=((Apply)), D=(void)): repro becomes 2, all controls unchanged, and the full existing suite src/test/machine.secd.scm passes against it: ((ran 13) (failed 0)). Checked tests/comments: the documented 7→5 divergence concerns which dump Jump captures, not how a void-dump program closure is applied; nothing documents the current void-branch behavior as intended, and it contradicts the machine's own status?-branch and Danvy–Millikin's program-closure application (abandon current context, reinstate captured dump).

#### [MINOR] `aux.machine.secd.scm:103` — curryfy silently misparses multi-body lambda as an application

(curryfy '(λ (x) a b)) yields the term ((λ (x) a) b): the 4-element list fails the two λ patterns (which require exactly one body form) and falls through to the n-ary application clause ((,rator ,rand . ,rands) ⊣ (pair? rands)), which treats the symbol λ as an operator and applies (drop-right sexp 1) to (last sexp). Unlike the other malformed inputs — (curryfy '(cond (p a))), (curryfy '(f)), (curryfy '()) all raise, and test/curryfy-clauses asserts they do — a superfluous body expression is silently rewritten into a semantically different program that then evaluates without complaint. Fix: add a guard clause ((λ ,formals . ,bodies) ...) before the application clauses that raises the match error (or curries a begin), so a λ head never reaches the application fallback.

**Evidence.** Repro (csi): (to-string (curryfy '(λ (x) a b))) => "((λ (x) a) b)" — an application of the truncated lambda, not an error. By contrast (curryfy '(cond (p a))) and (curryfy '(f)) raise as the test suite expects.

**Independent verification.** Read curryfy in src/aux.machine.secd.scm (~lines 88-105): both λ patterns ((λ (,x) ,body) and (λ (,x . ,ys) ,body)) match only 3-element lists, so a 4-element (λ (x) a b) falls to the n-ary application clause ((,rator ,rand . ,rands) ⊣ (pair? rands)) which builds Comb((curryfy (drop-right sexp 1)), (curryfy (last sexp))). Ran a repro script under the scratch repo with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi; output: "multi-body lambda => ((λ (x) a) b)" (no error), while "(cond (p a)) => RAISED: match error for ((cond))", "(f) => RAISED: match error for ((f))", "() => RAISED: match error for (())" — exactly matching the reviewer's repro. Bonus: (λ (x y) a b) => ((λ (x) (λ (y) a)) b), an even more scrambled silent misparse. test/curryfy-clauses in src/test/machine.secd.scm:278-287 asserts via ⊨/error that the other malformed inputs raise, confirming error-on-malformed is the intended contract; no test or comment sanctions multi-body λ.

### aux.stream.series.scm

#### [MAJOR] `aux.stream.series.scm:100` — series:×* scale zips series elements instead of scalar-multiplying, breaking bivariate products and series:○

In series:×* the `scale` argument to stream:convolution (lines 96-100) must multiply ONE bivariate coefficient `a` (a number or a series-in-y) elementwise over the rest-stream `b`. It is wrong in both branches: (1) when `a` is a promise, `((stream:zip-with series:×) a b)` ZIPS a's inner y-coefficients pointwise against b's elements — a category error that produces wrong coefficients; (2) when `a` is a number but an element of `b` is a plain number n, `((series:* a) n)` is applied to a non-stream, and since stream:car/stream:cdr of a non-pair return the value itself, it yields the INFINITE constant stream (a·n, a·n, ...) instead of the constant series (a·n, 0, 0, ...), contaminating all higher y-powers. Consequence: any bivariate product with series-valued elements is numerically wrong, and series:○'s bivariate branch (which lifts α's coefficients with series:const and multiplies with series:×*) silently returns wrong compositions. Fix: extract the element-product dispatch already written in `func` (lines 89-95) into a helper elem*, and define scale as (λ (a) (λ (b) ((stream:map (λ (bi) (elem* a bi))) b))); with this change all the failing cases below produce the correct coefficients (verified). Also note func's error message says "series:×" instead of "series:×*".

**Evidence.** csi repros: (1) ((series:* 2) 3) → (6 6 6 6 6) infinite repeat. (2) squaring q = (cons (series:const 1) (cons (series:const 1) series:0)) — i.e. (1+x) with lifted elements — via series:×* gives [x¹] = (2 1 1 1) instead of (2 0 0 0). (3) α = y + x (element0 = series (0 1), element1 = 1): (series:×* α α) gives [x¹] = (0 1 0 0) = y instead of 2y — (x+y)² comes out as x²+xy+y². (4) end-to-end through series:○: composing u² with β(x,y) = x + y·x² gives [x³] = (0 2 2 2 …) instead of (0 2 0 0) and [x⁴] = all zeros instead of (0 0 1 0). Re-running all four with scale redefined as (stream:map (λ (bi) (elem* a bi))) over b yields the correct expansions ((0 0 1)(0 2 0)(1 0 0) for (x+y)², (1 2 1) rows, etc.).

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.stream.series.scm (series:×* lines 86-102, scale at 96-100) and aux.stream.sicp.scm (stream:car/stream:cdr lines 31-43 return a non-pair unchanged; stream:convolution lines 180-189), then ran all four claimed repros with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi against the pre-compiled scratch repo. Every one reproduced verbatim: (1) ((series:* 2) 3) => (6 6 6 6 6) — infinite constant stream, because stream:car/cdr of the number 3 yield 3; (2) squaring (1+x) with series:const-lifted elements via series:×* gives [x^1] = (2 1 1 1) instead of (2 0 0 0); (3) (x+y)^2 via series:×* with α = (series (0 1), 1, 0...) gives [x^1] = (0 1 0 0), i.e. x²+xy+y² instead of x²+2xy+y²; (4) end-to-end through the public series:○ bivariate branch, u² ∘ (x + y·x²) gives [x^3] = (0 2 2 2) and [x^4] = all zeros, instead of the correct x² + 2y·x³ + y²·x⁴. I then implemented the reviewer's proposed fix (elem* extracted from func, scale = stream:map of (elem* a) over b) in a scratchpad clone: it yields the correct expansions for all four cases ([x^1]=(2 0 0 0), [x^1]=(0 2 0 0), [x^3]=(0 2 0 0), [x^4]=(0 0 1 0)) AND regression-matches the existing suite's bivariate expectations exactly (Pascal rows (1)(1 1)(1 2 1)... and the Riordan shape (1 0...)(0 1 1 1...)(0 0 1 2 3...) from src/test/stream.sicp.scm lines ~384-446). Scripts: scratchpad/verify-xstar.scm, verify-xstar2.scm, verify-fix.scm.

#### [MAJOR] `aux.stream.series.scm:147` — series:√/∞ silently ignores the constant term α₀

series:√/∞ hardcodes the initial guess 1 in its letdelay (Q = 1 + ∫(α'/(2Q))), which is only correct when α₀ = 1. For any other constant term it silently returns the coefficients of a DIFFERENT series (effectively √(1 + (α−α₀)) shifted), with no error. It also produces garbage for inputs like α = x (α₀ = 0, α₁ ≠ 0), where no power-series square root exists, instead of raising — note that the sibling series:exp does validate its precondition (α₀ = 0) and errors. Fix (verified numerically): compute q0 = (sqrt (stream:car α)) and use Q = (series:+ (series:const q0) (series:∫ (series:/ (series:∂ α) ((series:* 2) Q)))); additionally raise an error when α₀ = 0 but α₁ ≠ 0 (the existing both-zero branch that shifts by x² stays as is). This generalizes correctly and is backward compatible for α₀ = 1.

**Evidence.** csi repro: ((list○take 5) (series:√/∞ (list->poly '(4 1)))) => (1 1/2 -1/8 1/16 -5/128), i.e. the √(1+x) coefficients; expected √(4+x) = (2 1/4 -1/64 1/512 -5/16384). Independent proof: ((list○take 5) ((series:expt 2) (series:√/∞ (list->poly '(4 1))))) => (1 1 0 0 0) ≠ input (4 1 0 0 0). The proposed fix, run in the same session, gives (2 1/4 -1/64 1/512 -5/16384) and squares back to (4 1 0 0 0); on stream:1s it reproduces the current correct output (1 1/2 3/8 5/16 35/128) matching the existing test. Also ((list○take 5) (series:√/∞ (list->poly '(0 1)))) silently returns (1 1/2 -1/8 1/16 -5/128) instead of erroring.

**Independent verification.** Read /data/bitcoin/mnocentini-home/Developer/working-copies/aux.scm/src/aux.stream.series.scm lines 147-157: series:√/∞ builds Q = 1 + ∫(α'/(2Q)) with stream:1 hardcoded, i.e. initial condition Q(0)=1 regardless of α₀. Repro with the provided csi (scratchpad/sqrt-repro.scm): (series:√/∞ (list->poly '(4 1))) => (1 1/2 -1/8 1/16 -5/128) — the √(1+x) coefficients — and squaring it gives (1 1 0 0 0), not the input (4 1 0 0 0), so the function objectively fails to be a square root for α₀≠1. (series:√/∞ (list->poly '(0 1))) also silently returns the √(1+x) coefficients although x has no power-series sqrt. Verified the reviewer's fix (scratchpad/sqrt-fix.scm): with q0=(sqrt α₀) as the constant term it yields (2 1/4 -1/64 1/512 -5/16384) which squares back to (4 1 0 0 0); it reproduces the existing test expectations exactly for stream:1s (matches test/stream.sicp.scm:299) and for the Catalan identity (test line 482, output (0 1 1 2 5 14 42 132 429 1430)), and the both-zero x² branch still gives sqrt(x²)=x; sqrt(x) errors as proposed.

#### [MINOR] `aux.stream.series.scm:139` — series:○ bivariate zero-head check crashes on forced streams

series:○ decides between univariate and bivariate composition with (equal? β₀ series:0). This works only when the caller reuses the very series:0 object (as the tests do via (list->poly '())). A semantically identical zero head that is a different object, e.g. (stream:repeat 0), is rejected with the 'β₀ neither 0 nor series:0' error while unforced (equal? compares the two promise records' distinct closures → #f); and once both streams have been forced — which happens to series:0 after almost any use of the module — equal? descends into the memoized CYCLIC structure ((0 . R) where R is the same promise) and aborts with '(equal?) recursion too deep or circular data encountered', a hard crash with a misleading message. Fix: never run equal? on stream objects; select bivariate mode with (promise? β₀) (a stream in head position can only mean a bivariate series; zero-ness of an infinite stream is undecidable anyway), keeping (eqv? β₀ 0) for the univariate case, and error otherwise.

**Evidence.** csi repro: (define my-zeros (stream:repeat 0)); (series:○ geom (list->poly (list my-zeros (list->poly '(1 1))))) => error 'series:○' even though the same call with series:0 as head returns the Pascal triangle rows ((1 0 0) (1 1 0) (1 2 1)). After forcing both: ((stream:ref 3) series:0) ((stream:ref 3) my-zeros-2) then (equal? my-zeros-2 series:0) => 'Error: (equal?) recursion too deep or circular data encountered' (exit 70), i.e. the check itself crashes instead of reaching the intended error branch.

**Independent verification.** Read src/aux.stream.series.scm lines 134-145 (series:○ dispatches on (equal? β₀ series:0)) and src/test/stream.sicp.scm line 384ff (tests build the bivariate zero head only via (list->poly '()), which IS the series:0 object since list->poly = (list-> series:0)). Ran a repro script with the specified csi and scratch repo; output: "baseline rows: ((1 0 0) (1 1 0) (1 2 1))" (series:0 head works); "fresh zero head, unforced: (ERROR series:○ (β₀ neither 0 nor series:0 #<promise>))" (semantically identical (stream:repeat 0) head rejected); after forcing series:0 and the fresh zero stream, the same composition call yields "(ERROR recursion too deep or circular data encountered)" — the equal? check itself crashes on the cyclic memoized structure before reaching the intended error branch — and bare (equal? my-zeros-2 series:0) crashes identically. Exit 0 from the harness script; every claimed behavior reproduced verbatim.

### aux.sxml.scm

#### [MAJOR] `aux.sxml.scm:164` — math/m renders every real number as a broken mfrac

sxml-handler-math/m dispatches on (rational? v) BEFORE (number? v), but in CHICKEN (rational? 3) and (rational? 3.14) are both #t, so the (number? v) => (mn ,v) branch is unreachable for every real number. A plain integer (m 3) is rendered as a fraction 3/1, a flonum (m 3.14) as the fraction of its huge exact numerator/denominator flonums. Worse, sxml-handler-math/frac (line 160) emits its two operands bare — `(mfrac 3 1)` — instead of wrapping them in (mn ...) elements, so even the intended ratio case (m 1/2) serializes to <mfrac>12</mfrac>: mfrac requires exactly two child ELEMENTS, so this is invalid MathML that displays as the concatenated digits. Fix: guard the fraction branch with (and (exact? v) (not (integer? v))), and have math/frac wrap each operand through sxml-handler-math/m so numerator/denominator become <mn> elements.

**Evidence.** SXML->HTML->file! on `((displaymath (m 3)) (displaymath (m 1/2)) (displaymath (m 3.14)))` produced: <mfrac>31</mfrac>, <mfrac>12</mfrac>, and <mfrac>7070651414971679.02251799813685248.0</mfrac>. Also verified (rational? 3) => #t, (rational? 3.14) => #t in this CHICKEN build.

**Independent verification.** Read aux.sxml.scm lines 160-168: math/m dispatches (rational? v) before (number? v); math/frac emits bare operands with no (mn) wrapping. Ran /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi with the scratch repository: (rational? 3)=>#t, (rational? 3.14)=>#t, (numerator 3.14)=>7070651414971679.0, (denominator 3.14)=>2251799813685248.0. Repro script calling (SXML->HTML->file!
