- [category r1] Verified by execution: monad laws (left/right identity, associativity where applicable) for list, maybe, identity, reader, prob, difflist, writer-over-list, ziplist right-identity and diagonal semantics; difflist append/mappend composition; prob consolidation output; ziplist <*> matching the documented example. All bug findings were reproduced with the custom csi (scratchpad/review1.scm .. review6.scm). Not covered deeply: the aux.base match/pattern machinery itself beyond what these modules exercise; ziplist associativity edge cases with unequal-length inner lists (the skip-vs-truncate semantics on a short middle list, e.g. (>>= '(1 2 3) g) giving (a c) rather than zipWith-style truncation (a), is debatable design rather than a clear bug, so not reported); prob's use of inexact 1.0 vs the Haskell model's Rational (design choice, not reported); no benchmark of difflist vs plain list append (its O(1) mappend is correct by construction); aux.category.monad.plus beyond return/⊕, guard/monad and powerset/monad reading plus the existing passing test suite.

- [streams r1] Read all three modules in full and ran the full existing test suites as a baseline (stream-sicp-suite 12 tests, stream-series-suite 16 tests, stream-suite 5 tests — all pass). Numerically verified series division (tangent), composition (exp∘sin matches the known expansion 1,1,1/2,0,-1/8,-1/15), reversion (revert sine = arcsin: 0,1,0,1/6,0,3/40,0,5/112), inversion and sqrt via csi. Probed aux.stream.scm edge cases (take§ over-forcing via an error-trapped tail, stop§, zip§ uneven, append§/interleaved fairness, foldr§ strictness — the last is documented as deliberate in aux.anthropic.scm). Not examined deeply: the delimited-continuation yield§/map§/yielded machinery beyond the existing delimcc tests (depends on (aux continuation delimited), out of scope this round); the Monte Carlo / 2nd-order ODE numerics (already marked FAILING and commented out in the original suite); potential flonum-sqrt precision in primes/∞ for huge n (reasoned harmless: only perfect squares matter and CHICKEN's sqrt is exact there); stream:take-while's inclusive-of-stopping-element semantics (surprising but pinned by tests, treated as intended). Benchmarks were run under interpreted csi against the -O2 compiled modules; I did not rebuild the modules with the proposed dest/scan changes to measure end-to-end compiled deltas.

- [anthropic r1] Reviewed aux.anthropic.scm in full (all 1822 lines) with execution: rebuilt the scratch repo's aux.simdjson.so (it shipped unlinked against chicken-simdjson.o, so nothing importing (aux simdjson)/(aux anthropic) could load; relinked in the scratchpad with -I/home/mnocentini/.local/include per the memory note), ran the offline suite as baseline (110 ran, 0 failed), and did one live end-to-end curl-transport round trip against a local HTTP server (UTF-8 body, request-id header, temp-file registry all correct). Checked but found sound: the JSON validator's grammar itself (leading zeros, bare minus, truncation, trailing garbage), escape/flonum round-tripping, retry classification and jitter, wire close idempotence and double process-wait (memoized, verified), SSE framing edge cases (empty event field, CRLF, comment lines, data joined with newline), message validation, define-tool expansion, and the converse loop's stop_reason branches. Not covered deeply: the curl close thunk's kill-path against a genuinely hung child (only reasoned about), test/anthropic.scm's own correctness, and the chicken-simdjson.cpp/simdjson C++ side beyond how it affects this module's parse gate.

- [machines r1] All five assigned modules read in full; all five corresponding test suites run under the scratch repository (machine.env 4/0, machine.sc 5/0, machine.secd 13/0, schemer.seasoned 10/0, games.dice-of-doom 10/0 failures). aux.schemer.seasoned.scm and aux.machine.env.scm yielded no findings: the book ports were traced against The Seasoned Schemer semantics (intersect+all hop cases, comb-upto-last k-indexing, leftmost variants, rember1* skip printing) and the env chain (foldr shadowing order, hash-table-merge precedence, sentinel fresh/store discipline) matches its tests. The helper modules (aux.commons, aux.tabling, aux.stream.sicp, aux.continuation*) were read only as far as needed to reason about these five, not deep-reviewed. Dice-of-doom α-β pruning and move selection were verified by reasoning (fail-soft cutoffs cannot displace the argmax at the root because the root's upper limit is most-positive-fixnum and list-index ties break to the first, true, maximum) but not exhaustively tested; the negative-spare-dice reinforcement reachable only by calling game-tree directly with first-move #f and spare-dice 0 (as one test does) was judged unreachable in normal play and left unreported.

- [kanren-ext r1] All three assigned modules were read in full and exercised against the compiled scratch repository; the shipped suites (microkanren-arith 52/0, microkanren-date 20/0, microkanren-unionfind 19/0) all pass in this environment. Executed probes: arith — exhaustive plus° (0..12)^2, *° (0..8)^2, /° (0..20)x(1..8), the mirrored comparisons, divisor/multiple/square/composite/prime/even/odd/numeral/between/common-divisor in both directions, log°/exp° samples, backward plus°/*° stream closure; date — calendar kernel and weekday cross-checked against (chicken time posix) on 200+ pseudo-random instants plus leap/era edges, civil°/clock°/day+second°/month°/year°/next-month°/leap-year°/day-of-week°/weekday-midnight°/iso°/on-day°/span° in their advertised modes including invalid-date rejection; unionfind — reification shapes, backtracking (no state leak across cond°/§ branches: ≡ copies before mutating and path compression ↑! is only ever applied to the private per-answer reify union-find, so union-find state is backtrack-safe), conda/condu semantics, strings/pairs/aliasing chains in unify, deepening, and the reasoned sub-module's relations. Not re-derived line by line: the book-faithful Chapter 7/8 internals (adder°, split°, exp2°, base-three-or-more°) beyond suite + spot checks, the many measured-milliseconds claims in aux.kanren.date's comments (spot-checked a few), and the supporting modules (aux kanren micro, aux stream sicp, aux fds unionfind), which I read only as far as needed to judge these three.

- [fds-cont r1] All 8 assigned modules were read fully and exercised on the custom csi 6.0.1pre1 build. HANSEI probability mass was verified conserved (exactly 1, or the conditioned sub-mass) across coin/uniform/range/bernoulli/geometric models, depth-limited exploration with suspensions, re-exploration of suspensions, and probcc-variable-elimination equivalence; the grass-model posterior matches the test suite's expected 0.322/0.2838. An attempted optimization of unionfind's find via hash-table-ref/default was benchmarked and found SLOWER (0.32s vs 0.28s for 400k finds over 200k nodes), so no unionfind optimization is reported. Not covered deeply: (aux category continuation) monad beyond smoke-testing return/>>=; thread-safety of the global *meta-continuation* (single-threaded assumption seems intended); aux.judy/aux.lua were not part of my assignment.

- [cml r1] Read all 3576 lines of src/aux.cml.scm. Verified behavior with the compiled module (csi 6.0.1pre1): all three suites pass (test/cml.scm 113 cases, test/cml-lib.scm 31, test/cml-io.scm 79, 0 failures); a randomized 24-thread stress mixing channel offers with nacks, mailboxes, mvars, timeouts and ~1200 genuinely dying threads terminated with the invariant sent = got + nacked holding exactly (1183 = 1067 + 116); scaling benchmarks confirmed the header's complexity claims (channel wake-all, timeout heap, select-loop waiter cleaning, mailbox and spawn/join all linear; quantum-1/2/3 preemption-at-switch paths exercised). Probed and ruled out as bugs: negative timeout-evt not enabled at poll (documented deliberate in AUX-CML.md line 453), a closed raw fd among io-evt waiters starving the others (CHICKEN's file-select reports a closed fd as ready, so waiters wake), nack firing on a poll that raises (works), the mailbox-recv-evt captured-queue and channel do-thunk dequeue races (polls and commits of one sync are a single atomic region), and the dying-mid-switch redelivery paths (extensively covered by the existing suite). Hot-path profiling found no measurable optimization: an ivar poll costs 0.47us, sync on always-evt 0.56us, yield 0.9us, channel roundtrip 5.3us, and raising the quantum from 64 to 100000 changes throughput by only 1-6%, so the tick machinery is not worth touching; hence no optimization findings. Not exercised deeply: the tcp events under real network load/backpressure (only the suite's local echo/accept tests), the UTF-8 decoder against exhaustive invalid-sequence corpora (read carefully, spot-checked by the suite's split-sequence tests), TraceCML file destinations beyond the suite, and multi-branch port-input events across two ports in one sync. AUX-CML.md prose was consulted for contracts but not audited line-by-line.

- [core r2] All four assigned modules read line by line and exercised against the compiled scratch repository with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi. aux.variable.scm was fully probed (V type check, record printing with subscripts, equal? semantics, fresh distinctness, non-digit symbols) and is clean; its only edge is that (fresh () body) fails at expansion time, which I judged not worth reporting. In aux.base.scm I verified several suspicious-looking spots that turned out correct: mappair works because CHICKEN's map stops at the shortest list; the match/non-overlapping overlap detector, vector/record patterns, and the ,,x eqv? patterns all behave as documented; hygiene of the syntax-rules macros (let1, begin1, match/first, dmatch) is sound; length/>? with negative n misbehaves but overlaps the round-1 length/>? finding so I did not re-report. define-macro/define-macro-ir (aux.base.scm:26-37) are unused anywhere in the tree, so I only checked them statically. I found no benchmark-worthy optimizations in these modules beyond ones already reported or explicitly acknowledged in source comments (remove-duplicates O(n²) 'suitable for small lists', pairwise-different? hybrid threshold, memoize/arg split); the only measurable perf win found (cond/λ double evaluation of the O(n) position scan in secd's expression->de-bruijn) is reported as the cond/λ bug. aux.judy/aux.lua were not in my assignment.

- [streams r2] Read all three modules line by line and their suites (test/stream.scm, test/stream.sicp.scm). Verified series correctness beyond the suites by running csi probes: reversion of exp(x)-1 = log(1+x) coefficients, exp∘log(1+x) = 1+x, 1/(1-x), plus stream:>>= and stream:map tree mode — all correct. Checked and cleared several suspects: map§/yielded is for the yield/extract protocol (works, tested in test/delimcc.scm), stream:take-while's inclusive-of-stopper semantics is intended (test depends on it), the accumulator-based numbers/triangular is safe because memoized cons cells force in order, the plain (non define-delay) numbers/powers-of-2/∞ self-reference is safe inside cons§'s delay, and primes/∞'s flonum sqrt bound cannot misclassify (smallest factor is always ≥ 0.5 below √n). series:/ of two identically-zero series hangs silently (exit on timeout) but that is inherent to lazy series division (semi-decidable zero test), so not reported. Benchmarked and rejected two optimization candidates as immaterial: enumerate-weighted's per-comparison weight recomputation (13k calls, 0.02s for 5 Pythagorean triples) and series:*'s repeat+zip-with scaling inside series:× (~3% vs stream:map-based scale). Did not re-derive the internals of aux.continuation.delimited (yield§/resetcc) beyond confirming the stream protocol works, and did not stress multi-threaded forcing of shared module-level streams.

- [fds-cont r2] Covered with executed tests: 20k-op randomized model tests for fds-queue and sbral (cons/car/cdr/ref/update/->list/length — no invariant violations found); 50-trial randomized union-find vs naive component model incl. interleaved path compression, plus union-by-rank invariant check (all pass); nondeterministic chooseD/chooseB/assert/markD-cutD/lazy-promise/take semantics; hansei probability mass conservation for exact and depth-limited reify (V+C suspensions sum to 1), re-exploration of folded results, bernoulli/uniform/uniform-range/geometric/when/normalize/variable-elimination/λ-probcc-bucket/leaves; classic amb (triples, empty choices, sat-solve), trycc, trycc/named, escapecc, letcc/escaper, apply/cc; delimited reset/shift incl. multiple values, nesting, state monad, yield, delimcc-foldr; macro-hygiene probes of every exported macro from bare client modules (only pretty-print leaks). - [kanren-ext r2] All three modules read line by line and exercised with the custom csi against the pre-compiled scratch repository. aux.kanren.arith.scm: verified faithful to upstream trs2-arith (adder°, *°, split°, /°, log°, exp2°, base-three-or-more°, repeated-mul° all match), and executed the added relations' termination claims — multiple°/divisor° close (divisors of 12 answer 6 and stop), square° backward fails finitely on non-squares, composite° fails finitely on primes, common-divisor° closes, numeral° really enumerates 0,1,2,... in increasing order, and between° on an empty range with n fresh really is a closed empty stream (the comment is correct; I initially suspected divergence and was refuted by the run) — no new findings there. aux.kanren.unionfind.scm (+ reasoned): probed over-taking finite streams, empty streams, vector unification (documented limit), user-built (V 'x) collisions, string unification, conda/condu/unfair-append conditionals, rank-table consistency of the direct-bind path, and the ∨-shared union-find (safe: every ≡ copies before writing; reify only mutates a fresh table) — no new defects beyond the round-1 reify/status report. I implemented and benchmarked a ground-terms fast path for ≡ that skips the per-unification double hash-table copy: it was a wash (stacksort-5 63→73 ms, dyck-42 85→81 ms, interpreted) because the walk* pre-pass costs what the copy saves at realistic table sizes, so I am deliberately not reporting it. Not covered deeply: prime°'s 0..60 agreement (author-measured, expensive), the very slow documented open modes (day+second° with only d ground, clock° full-day enumeration), and stacksort°/2stacksort° semantics beyond the suite's own assertions.

- [cml r2] Read all 3576 lines of aux.cml.scm and cross-checked against AUX-CML.md (which documents nine prior internal review rounds, so several suspicious-looking spots — %atomic-cvar-set! not marking woken threads, negative timeout-evt firing at the next time check, barrier oldest-first wakeups — turned out to be documented deliberate decisions and were NOT reported). Ran and passed the full core/io/lib suites with the custom csi. Dynamic probing beyond the suites: negative/zero/past timeouts, wrapped-guard error paths, io edge cases (char-vs-line select on one port, n=0 input-string-evt, peek-then-read, input-all at eof), nack firing on sync/timeout-0 polls (correct), a 500-sleeper random-deadline heap stress plus a 20000-stale-entry purge stress (no early or lost wakeups), and a chaos test with randomly dying channel/mailbox consumers under supervisors (perfect message conservation, no duplicates — an apparent mailbox FIFO violation proved to be a test artifact of concurrent receivers). Benchmarked the hot paths (rendezvous 2.5us, sync send-evt 3.0us, 2-way select 5.0us, with-nack select 8.3us, mvar take+put 0.93us, input-event poll 13.4us): after the documented tuning rounds I found no optimization I could demonstrate as a clear win — candidates I measured and rejected include select* skipping %force-group for all-base events (only ~4% vs sync+choose), hoisting the per-poll handle-exceptions in %sync-on-grp (~0.21us/poll, ~5%, and the restructure risks covering do-thunks), and replacing (apply bytevector ...) in %socket-drainer (the apply form measured FASTER than a set-loop). Not deeply probed: the tcp layer beyond the existing suite (tcp-accept-evt races under real load), cml/execute's pipe-fd leak if process-fork itself fails (deemed too niche), and long-running scheduler-fairness properties of rdyQ2 promotion.

- [core r3] All four assigned modules read in full and exercised against the compiled scratch repo. aux.variable.scm is tiny and came up clean (record equality, printer, fresh/fresh₁ all behave; only nit is that (fresh () ...) is a syntax error, not reported). - [kanren-core r3] All three assigned modules read in full and exercised against the compiled scratch repo with /home/mnocentini/.local/bin/csi. Ran ~60 behavioral probes (scratchpad files r3-probe1..7, r3-bench1): occurs-check through pairs/vectors/records including mutual cycles, disequality simplification/subsumption chains across variable aliasing in both index orders, absento propagation through structures and late instantiation, type-constraint interplay in both assertion orders, soft-cut if° (including infinite condition streams), or°/and° interleaving fairness on infinite relations, deferred variables, all four aggregation macros (groupby°/window°/set°/enumerate°) including empty and unbound-key groups, take° with negative/zero n, record unification round-trips, tabling (memoize! recursion speed, define-tabled fresh:/store:, letrec-tabled mutual recursion, lambda-tabled, λH hidden-table access), and the show module's markdown/mermaid/profile renderers (outputs inspected by hand). No further correctness bugs found in these paths, corroborating earlier rounds. Not deeply re-examined: walk/projection micro-performance internals (covered by earlier-round findings on length/sbral hoisting and match/non-overlapping), the sxml renderer under (aux unittest) HTML integration (only smoke-level via markdown twin), and °->answers/profiled timing accuracy. The known-divergent soft-cut-over-infinite-failure case (if° with a condition that fails only after infinite search) was confirmed divergent but is semantically unavoidable and not reported.

- [ffi r3] aux.judy, aux.lua and aux.hwy got static review only: Judy.h and lauxlib.h are absent as stated, and Highway is also not installed on this box (no vqsort.h anywhere, the scratch-repo aux.hwy.so has an unresolved chicken_hwy_vqsort_int32 symbol, and src/aux.egg has the hwy extension commented out), so aux.hwy could not be executed either -- its binding is thin and nothing static-review-worthy surfaced beyond what execution would be needed to confirm (e.g. Highway's NaN ordering, which I did not assert without running it). The vendored timsort.c (CPython's algorithm) was reviewed only for the interface assumptions chicken-timsort.c relies on (reverse-slice stability trick confirmed at lines 1139-1145 and 1194-1195, non-zero return on merge_getmem failure), not line-by-line. aux.sxml's define-vhost/define-vhost-map were reviewed statically but not exercised against a live spiffy server (the two known route bugs are already reported); the SXML->HTML pipeline itself was executed and its output inspected. Everything else was executed: timsort across all type classes (fixnum boundaries, flonums with infinities, big ratnums stressing the pinned-C_i_lessp claim, strings with embedded NUL/UTF-8, symbols, chars, booleans), keyed/stability/reverse/inplace-vector-cycle-write-back under per-comparison GC, nested and escaping comparators; simdjson parse/load/round-trip including 64MB c-string inputs (no C-stack issue) and int64/uint64 boundaries. The earlier-reported simdjson error-path DOM leak family was deliberately not re-reported (near-duplicate of the round-2 'huge integer literal ... leaks dom' and 'simdjson-load leaks' findings).

- [machines r3] Executed heavy differential testing rather than only reading: (1) SECD: 1000 random cond-free lambda terms agree across all five evaluators (value, value₊, →/interpreted, →/compiled, →/compiled⁺) — no new miscompile class; 400 terms including cond only reproduce the already-reported Test-drops-control defect. One nuance worth passing to whoever fixes it: under compile⁺'s Enter/Exit inlining, even an If in tail position of a lambda body hits that defect (the dropped instruction is the Exit), while compile handles the same term correctly — so the fix must repair the Test transition (append the remaining control), not just non-tail call sites. (2) dice-of-doom: αβ (values and handle-computer's move choice) verified equivalent to plain minimax on 100 random 2- and 3-player boards — the pruning/truncated-ratings/list-index interaction is sound. (3) schemer.seasoned: rember1*/letcc, rember1*/try and all four leftmost variants verified against reference implementations on 3000 random symbol-only sexps (non-symbol atoms crash, but that is already reported); comb-upto-last and intersect+all traced by hand against their specs. (4) machine.env: hash-table-merge precedence and the stale-shadow behavior of E->alist are asserted by the module's own test suite (test/machine.env.scm lines 26-32), i.e. documented behavior, not reported. All five suites pass as baseline. Not examined deeply: the stream library internals that dice-of-doom leans on (covered by earlier rounds), and the ~800µs/node residual cost of lazy tree construction after both memo-table fixes (stream/closure overhead inherent to the design).

- [cml r3] Read all 3576 lines of aux.cml.scm and cross-checked suspect paths against the pinned SML/NJ sources (sync-var.sml, timeout.sml). Ran 8 probe programs against the compiled module: quantum-1/2/64 conservation stress over channels+select+nack, mvar mutual exclusion, ivar fan-out relay; death-during-switch recovery for a blocked sender (before-thunk raise -> receiver retries), a mailbox receiver (message redelivered) and a syncvar reader (value relayed); nack exactly-once in blocked-loss, enabled-commit and winning cases; run-cml continuation escape and re-entry; idle-loop CPU (1ms cpu for 1s sleep); negative/zero timeouts (faithful to ML's pollFn); IO layer (partial line survives a timed-out input-line-evt, UTF-8 split across writes, channel ports, write-string-evt, cml/execute round trip through cat, input-evt); port-close wakeups for readers (101ms) and mid-write waits; multicast ordering to two ports; RPC/state with exception propagation and server survival; TraceCML watch to a mailbox destination (parameterize not reaching the watcher is the documented isolation semantics, verified global assignment works). Hypotheses tested and disproven: EBADF poisoning of the shared io poll (CHICKEN reports a closed fd as ready, so waiters wake), %sleep-ms busy-spin, negative-timeout unfairness. Not covered deeply: tcp-accept-evt/tcp-connect-evt under real network load and multi-acceptor races, custom ports with maker-owned data slots (the linear %port-table fallback), latin-1/binary encodings on the write path, TraceCML file destinations across shutdown/reopen, cml/execute's env variant, and the CleanUp registry under concurrent registration; these were reviewed statically only. No new refactorings or optimizations reported: the duplication candidates I found are already covered by earlier rounds' findings (hand-off switch copies, nack-loop copies, %wake-waiter!) or are deliberate hot-path specializations (%sync-on-bevts vs %sync-on-grp), and no optimization survived with a demonstrable measured win.
